- **Gold set and metrics (arch10):** `gold.data.ts` holds 113 real players across eras and roles (1947-2025 ratings in `data/`), each with a role spec (what an acceptable build must promise) and the 1-4 closest builds his position allows that fit it. `metrics.script.test.ts` (`RUN_ARCHETYPE_METRICS=1`, `ARCH_EXPANDED=1`, `ARCH_SPOT=file` for the top-50 spot check) reports the gold set's hit rate, churn, share spread, poor fit per population and stability under a uniform rating shift; `archetypes.test.ts` asserts floors. arch9 -> arch10 (random league / real NBA 2016-2025 with stats; expanded ratings off): gold set 43% -> 54% of players get one of their listed builds, 56% -> 67% a build that fits their role (the floor in the test is 60% / 40%: this fork's real ratings often contradict a player's real-life role, mostly perimeter defenders and players before 2000, whose ratings show no defensive or role signal, so the asked-for 85% was not reached); churn 12.2% -> 6.1% of seasons (diag: 11.5% -> 5.6%); max share 2.2% -> 3.6% / 4.8% -> 4.6%, effective builds (exp entropy) 244 -> 232 / 174 -> 167; draftable prospects' max share 20.2% -> 5.9% (effective builds 19 -> 74); poor fit league 6.5% -> 3.2%, veterans 21.7% -> 7.7%, draftable prospects 5.4% -> 4.0%, real 2.0% -> 3.4%; same build after a uniform +-3 / +-6 shift of every rating but height 82% / 68% -> 81% / 66%; 0 hard position conflicts; level-relative audit mismatches 1.5% / 2.0% -> 3.2% / 2.8% (the blended reading names a few skills that stand out at his position or in his stats rather than in his own shape; raw undeveloped prospects read 20% on the league-relative audit since they are now read against prospect tables, draftable ones 4.5%). With expanded ratings: gold 42% -> 35% listed / 51% -> 57% role, churn 12.7% -> 4.9%, prospects' max share 20.9% -> 6.1%, veterans' poor fit 20.0% -> 7.9%, shift stability 78% / 60% -> 75% / 57%.
- **Calibration and diagnostics:** `calibrate.script.test.ts` (`RUN_ARCHETYPE_CALIBRATION=1`, `ARCH_EXPANDED=1` for the expanded tables) evens shares within each group with a small additive bias (-0.4..+0.25); `seeds.script.test.ts` rebuilds the regenerator's targets; `diag.script.test.ts` (`RUN_ARCHETYPE_DIAG=1`) audits each label against the ratings on the level-relative scale. arch8 -> arch9, random league / real NBA 2016-2025 (expanded off): labels 301 -> 346 (all 2K), hybrids 13.9% / 10.0% -> 0, level-relative audit mismatches 1.5% / 2.0% (the absolute-scale audit the old level-gated labels passed at 0.15% / 0.18% now flags 21% / 31%, by design: a low-level player with a shooter's shape is a shooter), descriptor agreement 54.2% -> 56.0% / 46.4% -> 60.5%, max share 2.9% -> 1.9% / 3.5% -> 4.8%, 0 hard position conflicts, poor fit 6.2% / 2.0% (veterans 20%, prospects 1.9%).
- **Stored per preseason:** the label is saved on the player (`p.archetypes`, one entry per season) when the preseason phase begins, after development, so it holds all season and his history of changes is kept (it travels in league files; only the current entry is shown, the per-season list is kept internally). New leagues get a first entry for everyone; older leagues and players without an entry fall back to a live reading (stored the first time the player page is opened). Prospects are always read live, since their ratings can be regenerated. With the switch off nothing is stored or computed.
- **Where:** the player page (_Archetype_ line with a why popover, replacing _Build_; no history is shown), Draft Scouting (_Archetype_ column), Advanced Player Search (_Archetype_ filter), the roster page (_Roster composition_ panel with balance warnings) and League → _Archetypes_ (`/l/:lid/archetypes`: counts and best players).
- **Regenerate ratings:** prospects can be aimed at an archetype (per prospect, or a class pool). It uses the same solver, so overall, potential and height stay exact; the preview shows the archetype the new ratings actually derive to (a check mark when it is the one asked for). The 447 builds are still available under _Advanced: build_.
- **Known limits:** a draft prospect's archetype uses scouting-fuzzed ratings (like skills and positions), so it can differ slightly from the true one (everyone else is read from true ratings, see Scouting fuzz); aiming at a very specific archetype succeeds about three to four times in five at a given overall (the class archetype pool only aims a prospect at archetypes his position allows). With the switch off, the Advanced Player Search still lists an (empty) Archetype column.

### Improved positions

- **What:** basketball positions (PG, G, SG, GF, SF, F, PF, FC, C) by **size band first, then role within it** (pos2; replaced the two-axis size/ball-skills model). Three readings of a player:
  - **Length** (inches): height (`66 + 0.27 * hgt`) plus 26% of the wingspan-minus-height gap (expanded ratings; none without), nudged by build: strength, rebounding (defensive 0.7, offensive 0.3; `reb` without expanded ratings), interior defense (`diq` without) and inside scoring push up, speed and perimeter defense down. Strength and rebounding weigh about a fifth of what they used to, so strong or rebounding guards stay guards.
  - **Ball skills**: passing, dribbling, three, speed, offensive IQ and perimeter defense, minus half his overall level (stars are good at everything), as a percentile of generated players.
  - **Playmaking** ("who runs the offense"): with minutes, assist% + usage%/10 of this and last regular season (by minutes; percentile of real NBA players with 500+ minutes), weighted up to 82% at 1,500 minutes and scaled down below; without minutes, passing, dribbling, offensive IQ and ball security (`drb` without expanded ratings), minus half his level.
  - **Band**: length minus 5.2 inches per unit of playmaking above the median: guard below 76.7, wing to 82.3, big above, with hard limits on height (under 6'4" never a big, 7'0" or taller never a guard). So a 6'6" primary ball handler is a guard, a 6'9"-6'11" point forward a wing or an F, not an FC.
  - **Role**: guards PG / G / SG, wings SG / GF / SF / F, bigs F / PF / FC / C (SG and F sit in two bands): the nearest centroid in (length, playmaking, ball skills), with a small per-position bias. Centroids, cutoffs and weights were fitted on the real players' listed positions (`bios.pos`, 2000-2015) and checked on 2016-2025 and on the 2025 stars. Everything is on fixed scales (inches and fixed percentile tables), so positions do not drift with the league.
  - Code: `positionRaw`/`positionAxes`/`sizeBand`/`improvedPosition`/`positionFor` in `src/common/archetypes/position.ts`; the worker passes his stats rows and last season's ratings (`pos.basketball.ts`, `develop.ts`, `augmentPartialPlayer.ts`). Diagnostics: `RUN_POSITION_FIT=1` on `positionFit.script.test.ts` (knots, shares, churn; `FIT=1` refits the biases); the real-NBA check is a test with a floor (`position.test.ts`).
- **Before -> after (pos2):** real NBA listed positions 2016-2025 (8,000 player-seasons, with the previous season's stats as a real-players league reads them): exact 37.0% -> 43.4%, exact or adjacent (one step on PG, G, SG, GF, SF, F, PF, FC, C) 74.3% -> 82.7% (expanded ratings 82.2%; BBGM's formula 36.8% / 79.3%; all seasons 1947-2025 72.2% -> 78.3%). 2025 stars: SGA F -> G, Luka F -> GF, Cade F -> GF, LeBron FC -> F, Durant FC -> F, Jokic FC, Curry PG, Giannis FC, Draymond FC -> F, Haliburton PG, Tatum FC -> F, Banchero FC -> F, Booker GF -> PG, Edwards GF -> G, Wembanyama FC -> C. Shares in a random league (sticky careers, ovr >= 38; old formula / pos2): PG 14.3 / 18.1, G 6.8 / 6.4, SG 7.0 / 9.2, GF 12.1 / 10.3, SF 17.3 / 14.6, F 16.4 / 15.1, PF 9.2 / 10.8, FC 11.8 / 9.9, C 5.2 / 5.7 (a generated player with no stats reads a little more often as a point guard). With expanded ratings a few stars on a band edge move one step (SGA GF, Durant FC, Curry G).
- **arch13 (fewer ratings-only point guards):** read from ratings alone (no minutes yet: a random league at creation, draft prospects, rookies), point guard's bias is `RATINGS_ONLY_PG` 1.2 lower, faded out as his stats take over (`fromStats` on the axes, 0..1 with the playmaking stats' weight), so a player with a full season reads exactly as before. Random league shares (sticky careers, ovr >= 38): PG 18.1% -> 14.2% (old formula 14.3%), G 6.4% -> 10.3%, the rest unchanged; real NBA 2016-2025 exact or adjacent 82.5% -> 82.6% (exact 43.8% -> 43.1%); the 2025 stars unchanged (SGA G, Luka and Cade GF, LeBron and Durant F, Jokić FC, Curry and Haliburton PG). Kobe 2006 and Ray Allen 2001 still read as G: their assist and usage rates are those of Curry and SGA (Kobe 2005 26.5% / 30.5%, Curry 2024 27.1% / 31.2%, SGA 27.8% / 32.8%), so no stats rule moves them without moving the 2025 star targets; their builds are now scorers' (Archetypes, arch13).
- **SG generation (sgfix):** sweep19 found a random league had too few shooting guards (5-7% of the rostered players at creation, 9% in sticky careers, against 12.9% of real NBA player-seasons 2016-2025 listed SG), and that the position model was not the cause (on the real players with their stats it gives SG 12.5% against 14.4% listed; raising SG's bias moves SGA off his G star target). The cause was generation: BBGM's three player types (`genRatings.basketball.ts`) are point (passing and handle 1.5x, 90% of the players 6'3" and shorter), wing and big (27% of the 6'3"-6'9" players, so a 6'4" was more often a big than a guard). Generated guards were small playmakers or wings; real SGs (6'3"-6'7", mean 6'5") shoot and score first with a moderate handle and a wing's passing (real listed SG, ratings: pss 43 / drb 47 / tp 53 / spd 55; PG 57 / 58 / 48 / 58), a profile no type produced. Fix, with Improved Positions on (off: the same random draws and ratings, exactly): a **scorer** type (jmp/spd 1.45, dnk 1.3, drb 1.1, pss 0.9, ft/fg/tp 1.5, oiq 1.15, endu 1.3) drawn by height band (`SCORER_MIX`: 5% up to 6'1", 30% at 6'1"-6'3", 75% at 6'3"-6'6" with bigs 27% -> 2%, 30% at 6'6"-6'7"); so that the league's talent stays put, wings of 6'6"-6'9" and the point type up to 6'1" have their type factors 25% and 10% closer to 1. Measured with `sgShares.script.test.ts` (`RUN_SG_SHARES=1`; new-league defaults; creation over 8 leagues, careers: 10 seasons of the population model over 2 leagues, rostered player-seasons, ratings-only playmaking; draft: top 60 by value of every class about to be drafted) — see the table below. Real NBA listed shares 2016-2025: PG 12.1, G 11.7, SG 12.9, GF 14.0, SF 7.9, F 9.2, PF 8.8, FC 12.3, C 11.1. GF stayed low and SF/F high (the position model's biases, fitted to the old formula's shares; re-fitted in fixes4, below). Mean ovr of the rostered players at creation 51.75 -> 51.99 (ratingInflation's first season, seeds 7 and 1: 51.75 -> 51.81). The position model, its biases and the real-NBA accuracy are unchanged. With the full engine (games and stats, so playmaking is read from assist and usage rates; one league, 10 seasons, rostered player-seasons): SG 5.1% -> 11.0%, PG 16.3% -> 14.1%, G 5.9% -> 8.2%. Archetypes (`metrics.script.test.ts` now samples the random league as a new league generates it, `ARCH_STOCK_GEN=1` for BBGM's types; norms unchanged), stock generation -> scorer type: gold 67.3% listed / 79.6% role -> the same, churn 5.6% -> 5.1%, max share league 3.1% -> 3.0%, prospects 6.6% -> 5.4%, real 4.8% (unchanged), poor fit league 3.2% -> 3.4% (within noise), veterans 8.6% -> 7.9%, prospects 4.2% -> 4.0%, real 4.9% (unchanged), effective builds league 227 -> 216. Recalibrating the norms on the new sample (`calibrate.script.test.ts`) was tried and not kept: gold 67.3% -> 56.6% and prospects' max share 5.4% -> 10.9% (left to the archetype rebalance).

  | Shares (%)                    | PG           | G          | SG          | GF          | SF           | F            | PF          | FC           | C          | Draft top 60 at 6'3" or shorter |
  | ----------------------------- | ------------ | ---------- | ----------- | ----------- | ------------ | ------------ | ----------- | ------------ | ---------- | ------------------------------- |
  | Real NBA listed 2016-2025     | 12.1         | 11.7       | 12.9        | 14.0        | 7.9          | 9.2          | 8.8         | 12.3         | 11.1       | about 16                        |
  | Creation, all players         | 15.0 -> 13.0 | 8.8 -> 9.2 | 6.5 -> 14.6 | 9.9 -> 7.5  | 15.3 -> 12.8 | 15.9 -> 15.9 | 8.1 -> 7.6  | 11.3 -> 10.4 | 9.3 -> 9.0 | 18.5 -> 17.3 (8 classes)        |
  | Creation, rostered            | 15.4 -> 13.7 | 8.7 -> 9.3 | 5.5 -> 12.7 | 10.2 -> 8.1 | 15.2 -> 13.1 | 16.3 -> 15.5 | 7.1 -> 7.0  | 11.9 -> 10.9 | 9.7 -> 9.8 |                                 |
  | Careers, 10 seasons, rostered | 14.1 -> 13.2 | 8.2 -> 9.1 | 5.6 -> 12.9 | 10.1 -> 7.0 | 13.9 -> 12.5 | 16.4 -> 16.0 | 10.8 -> 8.8 | 11.5 -> 10.6 | 9.4 -> 9.9 | 17.6 -> 17.3 (22 classes)       |

- **GF/SF/F re-fit (fixes4):** after the SG generation change GF was 7% of a random league and SF/F 13%/16% (the biases had been fitted to the old formula's shares). `POSITION_BIAS` and the guard band cutoff (`GUARD_MAX` 76.726 -> 77.33 inches; the wing/big cutoff unchanged) were re-fitted on the axes of random-league player-seasons against the real listed shares, with the real-NBA accuracy, the 2025 star targets and the unit-test fixtures as hard constraints; centroids and weights unchanged. Rostered player-seasons (population model, 2 leagues x 8 seasons, sticky), before -> after: PG 13.2 -> 12.2, G 9.1 -> 9.1, SG 12.9 -> 12.8, GF 7.0 -> 14.6, SF 12.6 -> 8.9, F 16.3 -> 11.3, PF 8.7 -> 10.0, FC 10.5 -> 11.2, C 9.8 -> 9.9 (real 12.1 / 11.7 / 12.9 / 14.0 / 7.9 / 9.2 / 8.8 / 12.3 / 11.1). Real NBA 2016-2025 exact or adjacent 82.8% -> 83.0% (exact 42.6% -> 42.6%); the 2025 stars keep their targets. G stays a little low: raising it moves Curry or Haliburton off PG. Archetypes read position as a prior, so wings that moved SF/F -> GF can change build.
- **Stickiness:** when a player develops he keeps his current position unless the best one is closer by a margin (0.3) both on this season's reading and on the average of this and last season's (last season's ratings row and stats), so one season's change does not flip him. Position changes per player-season: generated careers 5.7% -> 2.0% (no stickiness 7.1%); real careers 2000-2025 with stats (real ratings are re-rated every season) 20.6% -> 10.7%.
- **When positions change:** for new players at generation, and when players develop (preseason, or a God Mode edit). Rows of earlier seasons are never rewritten. Custom string positions (`p.pos`) are untouched. As before, positions come from true ratings (not scouting fuzz).
- **Archetypes:** the stored position is the archetype prior (hard blocks unchanged: same nine labels); prospects' live labels and the ratings regenerator read the position the game would assign. A G now reads with the shooting guards (`POS_BUCKET`: a G is a combo guard who is not the primary ball handler). pos2 recalibrated the archetype tables (norms off and expanded, seeds, stat norms, star bias with `STAR_SEASON_MAX` 3.5% -> 3%) and added the reading's role-fitting pick to seven gold lists. Metrics, arch11 -> pos2, expanded off: gold listed 64.6% -> 65.5%, role 76.1% -> 72.6% (big scoring guards such as Kobe 2006, Ray Allen 2001 and Booker 2022 now read as G and get playmaking builds); churn 5.6% -> 5.6%; real 2025 top 150 max share 3.3% -> 4.0%; max share league 2.4% -> 3.2%, real 2016-2025 3.7% -> 4.7%; poor fit league 2.9% -> 3.3%, veterans 7.3% -> 9.5%, prospects 4.3% -> 4.7%, real 3.6% -> 5.1%; +-3 / +-6 stability 80% / 64% -> 80% / 65%; 0 position conflicts (diag). Expanded on: gold 46.9% -> 44.2% listed / 63.7% -> 61.9% role, churn 4.2% -> 4.8%, real 2025 top 150 max share 4.7% -> 4.0%.
- **Setting:** on for new leagues, off for existing ones (off = the original formula, exactly). Turning it on in an existing league takes effect as players develop; mid-season, a player whose position is recomputed (e.g. a God Mode edit) may move to the new model before the preseason.

### Sim-calibrated ovr

- **Setting:** `ovrV2` (League Extras → Players, "Sim-Calibrated Ovr"). On for new leagues, off for existing leagues (off = the stock formula, byte-identical: `expandedRatingsOff.test.ts`). Turning it on or off recomputes every non-retired player's ovr (pot moves by the same amount) and value; league files and real-player leagues get their ovrs recomputed at creation.
- **What:** player ovr weighs every rating by its measured value in the game sim instead of the 2018 regression. The marginal-value study (`src/test/ovrFit/marginal.script.test.ts`: every game, every player on both teams gets an independent ±10 in every rating; OLS of the score margin on minutes-weighted exposure, 16,000 games per mode, with the new-league default game sim extras on: realistic possessions, technical fouls, perimeter defense, game sim fixes, position-aware team ratings, realistic rotations) gives the margin per game of a 36-minute player +10 in each rating. Ovr v2 before the fudge factor is `intercept + Σ weight × rating` with the weights proportional to those slopes (negative slopes → 0), rescaled so that over the rostered players of 12 generated leagues it has the stock formula's mean and standard deviation, plus a small long-run correction (`LONG_RUN` in the fit script: ovr also decides who teams keep, so a league run with v2 settles a little tighter and higher; measured with the population model under the new-league defaults); then the stock fudge factor and rounding. So the league's ovr scale, mean and spread stay put; height, speed, ball handling and three-point shooting count more, basketball IQ and endurance less; inside scoring and mid-range get the small weight the sim gives them (ins 0.01/0.05, fg 0.00/0.05 margin per +10 with expanded off/on, within noise of 0).
- **Expanded Ratings on:** its own fit on the expanded study (23 ratings perturbed independently): wingspan, perimeter and interior defense, offensive and defensive rebounding, ball security and catch-and-shoot/off-the-dribble get their own weights; the compat ratings the sim no longer reads (`diq`, `reb`) get 0, so nothing is counted twice (`fg`/`tp` keep theirs, the sim still reads them as the range ratings). Both variants are anchored on the same population (expanded ratings derived from the stock ones), so toggling Expanded Ratings doesn't move the league's ovr: mean shift 0.1, mean |shift| 1.7 a player (guards −2 to −2.7, bigs +1.8 to +2.5: the expanded sim values size and length more).
- **Player value:** with the setting on, `player.value` mixes in 10% PER-based value instead of 20% (ovr already measures sim impact; PER flatters box-score bigs).
- **Data-driven, re-fittable:** the weights live in `player/ovrWeights.basketball.ts`, generated by `src/test/ovrFit/fit.script.test.ts` from `src/test/ovrFit/marginal-off.json` / `marginal-on.json`. After a game sim change, re-run the study for both modes (`RA_SIM=off` measures the stock sim), then the fit, and re-check `LONG_RUN` (commands at the top of each file); `report.script.test.ts` (`OVR_REPORT=1`, `REPORT_MARGINAL_DIR` for another sim configuration's study) prints the distribution, correlation and positional residual tables. One set of weights for every sim configuration (fit on the new-league defaults; see the residuals with the sim extras off below).
- **Measured** (`report.script.test.ts`, 8 generated leagues, 3,120 rostered players; sim impact = Σ slope × rating, new-league default sim):

  | Variant             | Mean | SD  | p10 | p50 | p90 | p99 | Top 30 prospects | corr(ovr, impact) |
  | ------------------- | ---- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | ---------------- | ----------------- |
  | stock, expanded off | 51.4 | 9.2 | 38  | 52  | 62  | 72  | 38.0             | 0.958             |
  | v2, expanded off    | 51.0 | 9.7 | 38  | 51  | 63  | 74  | 39.6             | 0.988             |
  | stock, expanded on  | 51.4 | 9.3 | 38  | 52  | 63  | 73  | 37.7             | 0.950             |
  | v2, expanded on     | 51.1 | 9.7 | 37  | 52  | 63  | 73  | 38.9             | 0.989             |

  Mean sim impact at equal ovr by position, in ovr points (+ = underrated), PG / C: new-league default sim, expanded on: stock −3.6 / +3.3, v2 +0.1 / −0.1; expanded off: stock −0.8 / +0.6, v2 +0.1 / −0.3. With the sim extras off (stock sim, its own study): expanded on stock −3.5 / +3.8, v2 +0.2 / +0.4; expanded off stock −2.0 / +2.5, v2 −1.2 / +1.6. On the held-out rotation players of the study leagues, corr(ovr, on-court +/- per 48) 0.344 → 0.349 (expanded off) and 0.352 → 0.366 (on). Rating inflation population model under the new-league defaults (6 seeds × 30 seasons, seasons 6-30): league ovr mean / SD 50.25 / 10.07 with the stock formula, 50.14 / 10.23 with v2; with every extra off, v2 alone vs stock 49.8 vs 49.8 (before the long-run correction). Draft prospects and rookies are ~1-2 ovr higher (their low basketball IQ costs less).

### Realistic aging

- **Setting:** `realisticAging` (League Extras → Players). On for new leagues, off for existing leagues (off = stock development, byte-identical). Takes effect from the next development.
- **What:** from age 23 every rating gets its own target yearly change by age (`player/realisticAging.basketball.ts`); the shared base change (noise, coaching, work ethic, the secret aging curve of Player Traits) still moves all ratings together on top, so early bloomers, late bloomers and players who age gracefully keep their shapes. Up to 22 development is unchanged, so draft classes and random players are generated as before. Basketball IQ and free throws keep improving to ~29; shooting holds to ~31; speed and jumping decline gently from 28 and faster after 32, a little faster for big men (scaled from a wing's height to a center's); strength and endurance hold to ~30. With Expanded Ratings, the perimeter → interior defense and offensive → defensive rebounding drift stops after 31, so old guards no longer turn into interior defenders (idef and dreb used to rise until 36).
- **Measured** (`src/test/ovrFit/aging.script.test.ts`, 4,000 generated 19-year-olds developed to 38 with no attrition, ovr v2): peak age stock → realistic: oiq/diq 25 → 29, ft 25 → 29, pdef 25 → 27, idef 36 → 31, dreb 34 → 31; ovr vs peak at 31/33/35: −8.4/−13.9/−19.4 → −5.2/−10.5/−16.8 (bigs about 0.5 more); a 31-year-old guard's pdef/idef 39/39 → 40/38 and at 35 27/42 → 34/32. Aging curves (traits): early bloomers peak at 25, late bloomers at 29, graceful agers keep 7 more ovr at 34 than normal.
- **Long-run stability** (rating inflation population model, 30 seasons, 3-6 seeds, seasons 26-30): league mean ovr 49.8 stock, 50.1 with realistic aging, 50.1 with both new settings, 49.8 with both plus Expanded Ratings; with every League extra on 50.4 without these two settings and 50.2 with Realistic Aging only; under the new-league defaults (after the ovr v2 long-run correction) 50.1 with both vs 50.25 with the stock formula. Mean age of the top 10 players 27.0 → 27.4-27.7, top 50 26.6 → 27.2-27.6, best mean ovr by age 27.3 → 29, retirement age 31.3 → 31.1-31.3.

### Extended skill badges

- **Where:** badges after the original skills in player tables (roster, player ratings, player stats, free agents, draft, draft scouting, trade, trading block, watch list, upcoming free agents, advanced search), the player page header and ratings table. Legend and leaders: Frivolities, Player Rankings, _Skill Badges_ (`/l/:lid/frivolities/skill_badges[/:badge]`).
- **What:** 29 ratings badges computed from current (fuzzed) ratings, shown in exactly the same format as the original skills (plain `.skill` label; hover shows just the name; the legend page has the meaning and threshold): Cs catch-and-shoot, Pu off-dribble, Mr mid-range, FT free-throw ace, Sb stretch big, Hp high-post shooter, Fn rim finisher, Dk dunker, Tr transition, Fd foul drawer, Cl poised scorer, Is isolation scorer, Bu bully, Cn connector, Sf ball-secure, Ab ankle breaker, Pt post playmaker, St pickpocket, Bk shot blocker, Pa point-of-attack, Tw two-way wing, Or offensive glass, Dr defensive rebounder, Ph physical, Af athletic freak, Fit iron lungs, Hi high IQ, Hu hustler (also from a high hustle trait), Gg glue guy. Positive only, no warning badges.
- **Player traits badges** (only with the _Player traits_ setting on): Ch clutch performer, Rl Mr. Reliable (consistency), Im iron man (durability + endurance), Sd smart shooter (shot discipline + shooting), Cu cutter (off-ball), Ss screen setter, Dd disciplined defender (foul discipline + defensive IQ), Hh pressure-proof handler (handle + ball security), Lg late-game IQ, Ld leader, Rc quick recovery, Gr gym rat (work ethic), Bi big-game player, Tf team-first (ego), Ck cool head (temper). No badge for the secret aging curve. They use what the user can see: visible traits by their shown (scouted) estimate, hidden traits only once known exactly. Not added: vet savvy (traits already fold experience in). Calibrated to roughly 15-40 players per 30-team league each.
- **Combos (display only):** related skills and badges collapse into one named badge, no tiers. Level 1: PS pure shooter (3+Pu+Mr+FT), Mc maestro (Ps+B+Sf/Ab), Ib interior beast (Po+Fn+Dk), Bm board master (R+Or+Dr), Sp sharpshooter (3+FT), Sn spot-up sniper (3+Cs), Sm shot maker (Pu+Mr), Pk pick-and-pop big (Sb+Hp), Pf paint finisher (Po+Fn), Pw power finisher (Dk+Bu/Fn), FG floor general (Ps+B), Bw ball wizard (B+Ab), Fb facilitating big (Pt+Ps/Cn), **Sw switchable defender (Dp+Di), always shown when a player has both: no other combo uses Sw or takes Dp/Di first**, Rp rim protector (Di+Bk), Pl perimeter lockdown (Pa+St/Dp/Tw/Dd), 3D 3-and-D wing (3+Tw), Oc offensive crasher, Gc glass cleaner, Ex explosive athlete, Py physical specialist, Gt go-to scorer, Gl glue guy, Mo motor, Ce closer (Ch+Lg), Ih iron horse (Im+Rc/Rl). Level 2 (need **three** qualifying combos or family badges): Sc scoring machine, Dm defensive menace (never Sw), Pe playmaking engine, Pr physical marvel, Rg rebounding giant, Sv savvy pro (3 of Rl/Dd/Sd/Hh/Cu/Ss/Lg/Hi), plus Cp captain (Ld + Gl/Hi/Gg/Rl). One greedy pass: level 1 before level 2, largest first, ties in definition order, each part used once. The Skill Badges legend lists what every combo replaces. One function, `getDisplayBadges(ratings, skills, traits)` in `src/common/skillBadges/compute.ts`, does this for every view (via `player.format` `badges`; traits from `player.getBadgeTraits`).
- **Display:** combos, remaining original skills, then other badges. Up to 7 are shown, then a "…" in the same style; click/tap or Enter/Space toggles all of that player's badges (per instance, `aria-expanded`). No settings. Measured on three generated leagues with traits on, badges shown per player before → after combining: stars (top 30 per league by ovr) 22.8 → 6.4, starters (next 120) 5.4 → 2.9, bench 0.6 → 0.4.
- **Display-only:** not stored in `ratings.skills`, never used by the sim. Pure code in `src/common/skillBadges/`. Advanced Player Search: Bio field _Badges_ with "contains" (e.g. `Bk`, `Sw`).
- **Known limits:** cutoffs were calibrated on generated leagues. Clutch and injury-proneness are skipped (not derivable from ratings; the player traits cover them).

### Player, team and history pages

- Player pages: college rows (tagged _College_) in the stats tables, an _Earlier honors_ box, _Wrapped_ and _Storylines_ buttons, the _Archetype_ line, the _Traits_ line (and the _Traits_ sub-page), badges and the _Contract & Mood_ panel (collapsed by default).
- Team pages (_More_ links), League → History, History (all seasons) and Team History rows link to the Newspaper, Wrapped and Storylines for that team/season.

## Contracts, mood and player unrest

Unlike most of the pages above, these work in every sport. Switches are in League Extras; on for new leagues, off for existing leagues until turned on.

- **Options and partial guarantees** (_Contract Options & Guarantees_): the negotiation window has _Option_ (player option −5%, team option +10%) and _Guaranteed after year 1_ (100/75/50/0%) pickers for contracts of 2+ years. A partial guarantee is priced per season it covers, like an option: +10% × the part not guaranteed for each season after the first (a team option's season isn't counted again), so 0% guaranteed costs +10% on a 2-year deal (the same as a team option) and +40% on a 5-year deal; 50% on a 3-year deal is +10%. AI teams put terms in some of their deals too. Options are decided at the start of the re-signing phase: players opt out when the market pays more (or they're unhappy and wouldn't lose much), teams keep a team option if the player is worth about what he'd be paid. A declined option makes the contract expire then, so the player can be re-signed like any expiring contract. You can exercise or decline your own team option early from the player page. Releasing a player only owes the guaranteed part of seasons after the current one, and not an undecided team option. Contracts show a _PO_/_TO_/_%G_ badge next to the expiration year.
- **Extensions** (_In-Season Contract Extensions_): players in the last 2 seasons of their contracts can be extended in the preseason and regular season, from _Negotiate extension_ on the player page. The new seasons are added after the current ones, so this season's payroll doesn't change. Willingness comes from mood. AI teams extend some good players in their final season, but not into the luxury tax (soft cap) or over a hard cap, and try to settle a holdout with an extension (25% a day per team; into the tax only for one of the team's two most valuable players). Extensions can be undone like signings.
- **Mood transparency** (always on): the player page _Contract & Mood_ panel (collapsed by default with a one-line mood / willingness summary in its header; click the header to expand, and the choice is remembered in this browser; trade request and holdout notices stay visible) shows contract seasons with guaranteed amounts and options, release cost, a _What would make him re-sign?_ table (projected willingness if the team wins, after a deep playoff run, with a bigger role, with no more trades, or all of these), and mood history with sparklines. Mood is snapshotted at the trade deadline, at the end of the regular season and after the playoffs (up to 30 per player, in `p.moodHistory`).
- **Free agent demands** (always on): during free agency and the season, a free agent with no market comes down faster than the stock daily decrease (which is tiny next to modern salaries). Below replacement level (the median AI team's last-man value) he gives up half of what he asks above the minimum each day in the regular season (12% a day in free agency), so he's at the minimum within about 5 days. A better player who asks more than his share of AI cap space (the best free agent is matched with the most cap space any AI team with a roster spot has, the second best with the second most, ...) gives up 35% a day (8% in free agency, so about a week in the season), but not below that cap space, so players a team could still pay keep their price. Over-the-cap AI teams only sign minimum contracts in either cap mode; with no salary cap only below-replacement players drop fast. A free agent at the minimum still refuses a team he really hates (mood components summing below -8) until enough days unsigned bring him around. Code: `freeAgents/decreaseDemands.ts` (`getDemandMarket`); `FA_METRICS=1` on `decreaseDemands.test.ts` prints a day-by-day table.
- **AI re-signing and the luxury tax** (always on, soft cap or no cap, with a luxury tax): in the re-signing phase AI teams don't re-sign a player into the luxury tax unless he's one of the team's 3 most valuable players (draft picks are always signed). Teams kept drifting into the tax over a few seasons before (the AI only asked whether the team was better with him). The same with no salary cap (when there is a luxury tax), where the tax is the only brake: without it, a 15-season audit had half the league in the tax by season 6 and a team at 1.9x the cap and $430M in debt. AI extensions don't go into the tax either (the same exceptions for holdouts), and the random AI free agent signings that ignore a nonexistent cap (stock: a 2/#teams chance per team and day) stay under the tax line. After (seeds 1, 4 seasons): teams over the tax 7/12/12/15 -> 7/7/6/5. Code: `AVOID_TAX_MIN_RANK` in `phase/newPhaseResignPlayers.ts`, `aiExtensions` in `contract/hooks.ts`.
- **Free agent market** (always on): the Free Agents page _Market_ column shows how many AI teams are interested and the chance he signs elsewhere on the next day; click it for each team's interest (top target, next in line, fallback), the per-day chance and the most likely team. This mirrors how AI teams sign free agents (each day, a team that doesn't skip signs the best player it can fit).
- **Trade requests** (_Trade Requests_): very unhappy stars (top 15% by value) can ask to be traded, checked about weekly in the regular season. It's in the news, AI teams value him 15% lower when trading him away, and the trading block shows a _Trade request_ badge. AI teams actively shop a player who asked out (with _Need-Based AI-to-AI Trades_ on): see Trades. Requests end when he's traded, extended, or happy again.
- **Holdouts** (_Holdouts_, option _Holdout Frequency_, 0 for none): before the regular season, unhappy players paid well under their market value with 2+ seasons left may hold out, missing a few games (scaled to season length). An extension or a trade ends it early; holdouts also end with the regular season.
- **Data:** contracts gain optional `option`, `guaranteed` and `extensions`; players gain optional `moodHistory`, `tradeRequest` and `holdout`. Nothing needs migrating.
- **Known limits:** what-if projections hold everything else fixed. Market odds are for the next day only and assume teams keep their current needs. Holdout players sit out like injured players.

### Salary cap exceptions

_League Extras → Contracts → Salary Cap Exceptions_ (`featureCapExceptions`, on for new leagues, off for existing ones). Soft cap only: with a hard cap or no cap the switch does nothing. Stock BBGM lets an over-the-cap team sign free agents only for the minimum; this adds NBA-style exceptions. Code: `src/worker/core/contract/capExceptions.ts` (rules), `src/common/capExceptions.ts` (amounts, shared with the UI). Tests: `capExceptions.test.ts`.

- **Exceptions** (amounts are shares of the salary cap, so they scale with it and with inflation; lengths are capped by the league's max contract length):
  - _Mid-level_: 9.12% of the cap, up to 4 years. Not past the first apron (1.045 × the luxury tax line): a team close to it can only use as much as fits.
  - _Taxpayer mid-level_: 3.68%, up to 2 years, up to the second apron (1.105 × the tax line). It's the same slot as the mid-level exception (using either uses both), and it also uses up the bi-annual exception that year.
  - _Bi-annual_: 3.32%, up to 2 years, not past the first apron, and not in two league years in a row.
  - _Room_: 5.68%, up to 3 years, for a team that signed a free agent above the minimum with real cap space this league year (more space than the mid-level exception, so topping off a few hundred thousand of space doesn't cost the exceptions); it then has only this one.
- **League year:** exceptions reset when free agency starts (the league year is the first season of a contract signed now, `getContractStartSeason`), so free agency and the following preseason and regular season are one year.
- **Signing:** a free agent signing above the minimum that doesn't fit under the cap uses an exception automatically: the smallest one that covers the amount and length. Minimum contracts, re-signings (Bird rights) and extensions never need one. Uses are stored on the team (`t.capExceptions`), the contract (`exception`) and the signing event, whose transaction text ends "using the mid-level exception". Undoing a signing gives the exception back.
- **Length:** each exception has a maximum length (mid-level 4 years, taxpayer mid-level and bi-annual 2, room 3). When a free agent asks for more years than the exception that would cover his amount allows (5 years on the 4-year mid-level), the Free Agents page's _Sign_ button becomes _Sign 4y_: the longest contract an exception allows, at his price for that length (more per year for fewer years, the negotiation's own curve, `contractNegotiation/contractAmountForYears.ts`), with a tooltip saying so. If even that price is more than the exception covers, the button is disabled and the row says "Too long for exception" (reason on hover). In the negotiation window the rows that are too long say "Too long for your mid-level exception (up to 4 years)" on the row itself, not only in the disabled button's tooltip (`getSigningAdvice`, `generateContractOptions` `exceptionTooLong`).
- **User UI:** Free Agents (summary under the cap space line; _Sign_ buttons and the "can afford" filter count the biggest exception like cap space), the negotiation window (exceptions left, and each contract row that would use one says "Uses your mid-level exception"; rows that don't fit show why, e.g. "only allows up to 4 years") and Team Finances (exceptions left for any team).
- **AI teams** (`makeAiCapExceptionPicker`, used by `autoSign` through `getBest`): an over-the-cap AI team uses an exception for a free agent who would be among its top 10 players, shortening the contract to what the exception allows. Rebuilding teams only use them on players 27 or younger. Only contending teams go into the luxury tax with an exception, and only for a player who'd be in their top 8, and not with a tax-averse GM (_AI GM personalities_ `avoidTaxRankShift < 0`; tax-tolerant GMs go deeper into the roster). The free agent demand market (`getDemandMarket`) counts a team's biggest usable exception like cap space, so mid-tier free agents no longer drop to the minimum just because every team is over the cap. The Free Agents _Market_ column includes exception signings.
- **Repeater Luxury Tax** (option `capExceptionsRepeaterTax`, off by default): a team that paid the luxury tax in at least 3 of the previous 4 seasons pays it at 5/3 of the rate (2.5 instead of 1.5). Team Finances and the roster page show the higher projection; the tax event says "at the repeater rate". Works with no cap too.
- **Stretch Provision** (option `capExceptionsStretch`, off by default): when you release a player, a second dialog offers to spread what's still owed after the current season (only the guaranteed part, no undecided team option) evenly over twice those seasons plus one. In the regular season the current season stays as it is; before or after it, everything left is stretched. AI teams over the luxury tax stretch the players they cut. The contract gets `stretched: true` and the release text says so.
- **Data:** optional `t.capExceptions` (`uses`, `capSpaceSeason`; uses older than last league year are pruned), optional contract `exception` / `stretched`, optional event `exception`. Nothing needs migrating; with the switch off nothing is written. `t.capExceptions` is kept when a league file is loaded.
- **Measured** (AI GM audit, `AI_CAPEX=0` vs on, every other extra on, seeds 1/2 × 6 seasons, 30 teams, per-season means): exception signings per season 16.8 (mid-level 6.8, taxpayer mid-level 2.8, bi-annual 6.2, room 1.1); free agent signings 38.3 → 45.0, above the minimum 24.7 → 29.9, free agent salary per season 1.52 → 1.83 caps league-wide; mean contract 7.41% → 7.32% of the cap, mean payroll 1.03 → 1.03 caps, teams over the tax 4.7 → 3.8, over the cap 15.2 → 17.3, luxury tax paid $96.5M → $99.0M, win% sd 0.17 → 0.18, team ovr sd 12.2 → 13.2, re-signings 36.5 → 41.3, AI-AI trades 14.5 → 17.0. So mid-tier free agents get paid by over-the-cap teams without pushing salary levels or tax bills up.
- **Known limits:** no sign-and-trade, disabled player or trade exceptions, and the aprons don't restrict trades. The repeater tax counts only seasons with luxury tax records (`teamSeason.expenses.luxuryTax`).

## Trades

Unlike most of the pages above, these work in **all sports** (the trade code is shared).

- **Why the AI said no** (_Trade Explanations_): on the Trade page, when the AI rejects a proposal or answers _What would make this deal work?_ with a counter, a _Why?_ link under the GM's message lists, per asset, the terms the AI used: talent level, age and strategy (rebuilding teams value youth and picks more, contenders discount potential), contract (overpaid/bargain/expiring; rebuilding teams weigh contracts more), injuries, projected pick position, plus team-level terms: strategy, roster fit (team ovr before → after) and payroll/cap space. Built from `ValueChangeCalculator.explain()`, the same math the AI decides with.
- **Need-driven AI-to-AI trades** (_Need-Based AI-to-AI Trades_): contenders buy veterans in the run-up to the trade deadline, rebuilding teams sell veterans for picks and young players, and teams paying luxury tax dump contracts into teams with cap space (with draft picks as sweeteners). Over-the-cap buyers send back salary filler. A team whose star has an active trade request shops him to the other AI teams (contenders and winning teams likelier; the buyer keeps only its 2 best players off the table), and the trade text says the request was granted. When a buy/sell or trade request deal is blocked only because the buyer can't send enough salary back, half the time a third AI team with cap space takes the buyer's salary filler (for one of the buyer's picks if it wants one) while the seller is paid in picks (_Three-Team Trades_ on; every team must like its part and the cap rules hold for each team's totals). Recorded as one trade event per leg with a "Three-team deal" note. The reason is shown in the trade's transaction text. Off gives the classic random trades only; _AI-to-AI Trades Factor_ still sets the overall number of attempts.
- **AI offers to you / Trade Offers** (_AI Trade Offers To You_, options _AI Trade Offers Frequency_ and _AI Trade Offers Expiration_): replaces the stock _Trade Proposals_ page with a _Trade Offers_ inbox, `/l/:lid/trade_offers` (menu _Players → Trade Offers_, trade "More" links; `/l/:lid/trade_proposals` redirects there while the feature is on, and with it off the stock Trade Proposals page and menu item are back and `trade_offers` redirects the other way). AI teams send offers on their own: daily during the regular season (until the trade deadline) and free agency (chance frequency/10 per day), and at the start of the draft lottery, after-draft and re-signing phases (chance frequency/2). About 60% of the time an offer is for something on your trading block (when it has anything), otherwise an unsolicited ask from the team's strategy (contenders want good veterans, rebuilders young players and picks), filled out with `makeItWork` (AI adds only its own assets, pick protections allowed). An offer is only sent if it isn't lopsided against you by the AI's own math from your team's side (value change of at least −2, the point where the trade explanation stops calling a deal "close"; −4 for something on your trading block), so the daily tries go to another team instead (`MIN_USER_DV_UNSOLICITED`, `MIN_USER_DV_TRADING_BLOCK`). Each new offer gets a notification (no inbox message, since unread messages block play). Each offer shows the team, what you give/get, its source (_Trading block_, _Unsolicited_, _You asked_), a value hint from your side (good / roughly fair / favors them) and when it expires; buttons _Accept_ (goes through the normal trade proposal, undoable), _Negotiate_ (loads it on the Trade page, pick conditions kept) and _Decline_ / _×_. _Request offers_ (the old trading block "what would you give") asks every AI team about the selected players/picks and keeps the 3 best answers in the inbox. Offers expire after _Expiration_ days (your games in the regular season, days of free agency), when the phase changes, when the AI team loses a player, or when an asset in it moves; at most 8 are kept, one per team. Stored in the `aiTradeOffers` game attribute (new fields `daysLeft`, `rosterKey`, `source` are optional, so old leagues load as is). No unsolicited offers when AI trades are off (factor 0), in spectator mode, auto play or the no-trades challenge. Code: `trade/aiOffers.ts`, `views/tradeOffers.ts`, `ui/views/TradeOffers.tsx`.
- **Trade Market** (_Trade Market_): _Players → Trade Market_, `/l/:lid/trade_market`, linked from the Trade page, Trade Proposals / Trade Offers and the "trade deadline is coming" notification (a week before the deadline). Lists players each AI team is willing to move (0–100, from strategy, age, contract and role on the team, with reasons) and an asking-price hint relative to the team's own next first round pick.
- **Three-team trades** (_Three-Team Trades_): `/l/:lid/trade_three/:tid1-:tid2`, linked from the Trade page (_3-team trade_). Send any player or pick from any team to any other team. Each AI team must like what it gets (with the same _Why?_ explanations) and the salary cap rules apply to each team's total incoming and outgoing salary; during the season rosters can't go over the maximum. An accepted trade is recorded as one regular trade event per pair of teams that exchanged something, each noting it was part of a three-team trade, so trade summaries and transactions work as usual. Undo is supported. The regular 2-team Trade page is unchanged. AI teams also make rare three-team trades among themselves (see _Need-driven AI-to-AI trades_).

### Rumors

_Rumors_ (`featureRumors`, option _Rumor Notifications_): a rumor mill, `/l/:lid/rumors`, a tab of _Transactions_ (menu _League → Rumors_, trade "More" links), a _Rumor Mill_ card on the dashboard (your team's rumors first, highlighted) and a _Rumor mill_ sidebar in the newspaper's Transactions section (league and team editions, this season's rumors and how they turned out).

- **Where rumors come from.** Only from what AI teams are actually set up to do (src/worker/core/rumors): need-based sellers' veterans, salary dumps and trade requests (aiNeeds.ts), the Trade Market's most available players, buyers calling about sellers' veterans, buyers' own first round picks, stars whose mood is near the trade request line and underpaid unhappy players before the season (holdouts), expiring players an AI team would extend, the free agent market (each AI team's top target, next in line, fallback), head coaches on the hot seat (with the season in progress), AI GMs whose job security would get them fired, and AI teams' positional draft needs. About the user's team, only other teams' interest in his players (when AI trade offers are on) and his players' mood, never the user's own plans.
- **Confidence and outcomes.** Each rumor has a chance to come true (long shot, speculative, solid, strong). The gatherer scores it from the same rules the AI uses (deadline window, per-day signing chances, firing probabilities, ...; the GM hot seat is judged exactly as `gm.runOffseason` will judge it, with the honeymoon, the inherited-roster path, rebuild credit, patience, contender and market leash of fair GM job security). That raw score goes through a monotone recalibration table (`common/rumors/calibration.ts`, isotonic regression fitted on the audit, per type and per kind with 150+ rumors: e.g. deadline sellers' veterans almost never move, salary dumps and rebuild veterans more often than the rules suggest, extensions over the tax are their own kind), then the league's own track record per type corrects it on the odds ((confirmed + 60) / (model's expected + 60), 0.5× to 2×; measured against the model's chances, stored as the tally's 4th entry, so a correction that works doesn't undo itself). The rumor keeps its raw score (`raw`). The wording hedges with the confidence. It is marked _Confirmed_ (traded, extended, asked out, signed there, fired, took that position, ...) or _Didn't happen_ when its window ends (trade deadline, start of the next regular season, the draft, the end of the season). The page shows the track record by confidence, type and season.
- **Calibration** (src/test/ratingInflation/rumors.script.test.ts, 16 leagues × 6 seasons, every extra on; claimed = mean confidence, observed with its 90% Wilson interval; refit with `RUMOR_FIT=1`). Before: seeds 1-16 on the old model; after: fresh seeds 201-216, not used for the fit.

| Label       | Before n | Before claimed → observed (90% CI) | After n | After claimed → observed (90% CI) |
| ----------- | -------- | ---------------------------------- | ------- | --------------------------------- |
| Strong      | 567      | 69 → 54 (51-58)                    | 418     | 68 → 64 (60-68)                   |
| Solid       | 2683     | 47 → 45 (43-46)                    | 2773    | 46 → 46 (44-47)                   |
| Speculative | 2111     | 24 → 23 (21-24)                    | 3021    | 22 → 22 (21-23)                   |
| Long shot   | 2325     | 7 → 11 (10-12)                     | 1928    | 7 → 6 (6-7)                       |

| Type            | Before n | Before          | After n | After           |
| --------------- | -------- | --------------- | ------- | --------------- |
| On the block    | 1145     | 19 → 22 (20-24) | 1349    | 18 → 18 (16-19) |
| Trade interest  | 364      | 4 → 1 (0-2)     | 338     | 2 → 1 (0-2)     |
| Draft pick      | 208      | 5 → 9 (6-12)    | 243     | 6 → 7 (4-10)    |
| Unhappy player  | 905      | 32 → 35 (32-37) | 899     | 35 → 34 (31-36) |
| Extension talks | 841      | 40 → 34 (31-37) | 920     | 31 → 27 (25-30) |
| Free agent      | 1358     | 26 → 25 (23-27) | 1369    | 23 → 23 (21-25) |
| Coach hot seat  | 1142     | 39 → 40 (38-42) | 1317    | 40 → 42 (40-44) |
| GM hot seat     | 462      | 32 → 29 (26-33) | 419     | 40 → 41 (37-45) |
| Draft target    | 1261     | 41 → 35 (33-38) | 1286    | 39 → 37 (35-40) |

Before, strong rumors of every kind were overconfident (extensions 73 → 58, coaches 67 → 56, GMs 65 → 43, unhappy stars 64 → 41) and long shots underconfident (rebuild and salary dump sellers 6 → 20). The GM hot seat still used the pre-fair-job-security score; with it judged like the owner judges, uncorrected strong GM rumors were underconfident (66 → 80), now 73 → 71 (63-78). After, every label and every type × label cell is inside its interval except extension talks (solid 48 → 38, speculative 23 → 19), which vary from league to league more than a binomial interval allows (the fitting leagues had them on target). Scouting splits (`getScoutingSplit`) are opinions with nothing to confirm, so they are never rumors: see _Scouting buzz_ below.

- **How many.** About 3 a week in season, about 10 a week in the last 12% of the season before the trade deadline, a burst at each phase, several a day early in free agency.
- **No effect on the sim.** It only reads the league, from its own seeded random stream (league rumor seed + day counter; while it runs Math.random is swapped for that stream so no helper can draw from the sim's). A season played with it on and off gives the same rosters, contracts, records, coaches, events and the same next random numbers (src/worker/core/rumors/rumors.test.ts). Same seed, same rumors.
- **No leaks.** The text has no numbers but a pick's year, no ratings, no traits, and draft rumors name the position a team needs, never a prospect.
- **Scouting buzz** (only with _Scouting Reports_ on too): "Scouts are split on X: one sees a quality starting big, another a starting big", from `getScoutingSplit` (four other teams' lead scouts, each from what is publicly visible plus his own error). A separate, clearly labelled category in the feed (yellow _Scouting buzz_ badge, the high and low scout, "opinion, not graded"), with no confidence and no status; the type filter has _Scouting buzz_ and _Rumors only_. Stored apart from the rumors (`rumors.buzz`), so it never enters the track record, the calibration tallies or the confidence table. About the top 40 of the next draft class and the 30 best players 24 and under (better players more often); a player at most once per window (each third of the regular season, the run-up to the draft, the offseason), each window asking other departments. Rate: 0.3 a day in season, 0.6 in the playoffs, 1 when the regular season starts, 3 at the draft lottery and 2 at the draft, capped at 3 in any 7 rumor days (6 from the playoffs through the draft). In the test season: 18 items, 11 in the regular season and 7 from the playoffs through the lottery. Its own seeded stream: the rumors are the same with it on or off.
- **Storage.** The `rumors` game attribute: the current and last season's rumors (at most 500) and scouting buzz (at most 150), and the track record for 20 seasons. Off: nothing is made or stored.

### Draft pick protections and swaps

_Draft Pick Protections & Swaps_ (`featureDraftPickConditions`, all sports; on for new leagues, off for existing ones).

- **Adding a condition:** on the Trade page, every pick in the trade summary has a _+ protection/swap_ link (on the 3-team trade page, once the pick has a destination). Choose:
  - **Top-N protected** (1st round picks only): if the pick lands in the top N of its round, the team that traded it keeps it. _Then next years_ is the protection for each following year (e.g. `8, 5`), and _After that_ is what happens when the last one is protected too: the next year's pick conveys **unprotected**, it **converts to a 2nd round pick** (next year's 2nd rounder of the same team) or it is **extinguished**.
  - **Swap rights only**: the pick stays where it is, the receiving team gets the right to swap its own pick in that draft and round for it, if it's better.
- **Where it shows:** pick descriptions everywhere (trade asset lists, summaries, trading block, proposals, Draft Picks page with a badge; swap rights a team holds are listed on its Draft Picks page), the transaction/news text and Trade Summary page of the trade ("top 10 protected (then top 8, then unprotected)", "the right to swap 2027 1st round picks").
- **Resolution:** when the draft order is set (after the lottery, or at the start of the draft), swaps are executed if the other pick is better, protected picks convey or go back to the original team, and the obligation rolls over to the next season's pick of the same team (or converts/extinguishes). Each outcome is a news event (type `draftPickCondition`) with a notification for your team. If the pick a protection would roll over to was traded away in the meantime, the obligation is extinguished (noted in the event).
- **AI:** conditional picks are valued by their expected value: the chance of each pick slot (projected standings, simulated season odds and lottery odds, as for any pick) is split into conveyed/protected, with a discount for what's owed instead (a rollover is next season's pick valued the same way with the next protection in the list, times 0.85 for the wait, so a pick protected nearly every season is worth next to nothing; unprotected next year 75%, 2nd rounder 12%, extinguished 0). Swap rights are worth E[max(own pick, other pick)] − E[own pick]; a pick carrying someone else's swap right is worth E[min]. When the AI rejects an offer that includes its own 1st round picks, _What would make this deal work?_ first tries protecting them (top 3/5/8/10/14, one rollover year, then unprotected) and proposes the smallest protection that works.
- **AI trades with conditions:** in AI-AI trades (random and need-based), AI trade offers to the user and when it fills out a trade, an AI team may give its own picks with conditions: each step, up to two of its picks also get a variant with a protection drawn from a menu (top 3 → top 3 then unprotected; top 4 → 2nd rounder; top 5 → 5, 3 then unprotected; top 8 → 5 then 2nd rounder; top 10 → 2nd rounder; top 10 → 8 then extinguished; top 14 → 10, 5 then unprotected; only tops below half a round) and, when the partner owns its own pick in that draft, a swap-rights-only variant. They compete with every other asset on value, so they are used when they fit the balance best; rebuilding teams prefer the protected version (counted as if 40% smaller dv). Offers show the condition in the pick description and keep it when you open the offer on the Trade page. The Trade Market's asking price adds how much a top 5 protected first from you counts for compared to an unprotected one. Code: `trade/makeItWork.ts` (`aiPickConditions`, `getConditionVariants`).
- **AI asks for terms on your picks:** when the AI receives picks with conditions from the other team (Trade page _What would make this deal work?_, trading block / AI offers, AI-AI trades), `makeItWork` also tries changing the terms on those picks as candidate assets, valued like any other: when it wants more, loosening a protection the other team added (dropping the last rollover year, falling back to unprotected instead of a 2nd rounder/extinguished, lowering top-N to 8/5/3/1, or removing it); when it's getting more than it needs and the user's side can change, accepting a top 3/5/10 protection on an incoming 1st. The usual forward selection picks the smallest change that balances. The Trade page message says what changed ("They want the top 10 protection on your 2027 1st round pick lowered to top 5.", "...removed.", "They'll accept top 5 protected on ..."), and offers show/keep conditions on your picks (`tradeFor` `userPickConditions`). Only conditions added in the trade can change (existing ones on stored picks can't). Code: `getPickTermsVariants` in `trade/makeItWork.ts`, `getProtectionLoosenings`/`pickConditionChangeText` in `common/draftPickConditions.ts`, `getPickTermsMessages` in `trade/makeItWorkTrade.ts`; tests in `trade/pickTerms.test.ts`.
- **Changing a protection already on a pick:** a pick with a protection from an earlier trade can have that protection lowered or removed when it moves again, if the team holding the protection (the one that keeps the pick when protected) is also in the trade and isn't the one receiving the pick. In practice that's a 3-team trade; in a 2-team trade that team is never a party, so stored protections are only valued, not changed. In a trade it's a condition with `amend: true` (top 0 = removed). Valued with the new protection and the protecting team as fallback owner, applied in `processTrade` (text "protection changed to ..."/"protection removed"), and undo restores the old protection (not once the draft order is set). Validation: `validatePickConditionSpec(spec, dp, n, { participants, toTid })`.
- **3-team "What would make this work?":** button on the 3-team trade page. AI teams that don't like the trade ask for the smallest changes to the terms of picks they receive: loosening protections added in the trade, and loosening stored protections held by your team (not ones held by another AI team, which would lose value it isn't paid for). Greedy, up to 8 steps: a change that makes every AI team accept with the smallest gain, else the smallest change that gets the unhappy teams closer. The page shows "Team: We want the top 10 protection on your 2027 1st round pick lowered to top 5." etc.; a changed stored protection has a _keep existing_ link to undo the ask. Code: `makeItWorkMulti` in `trade/multiTeam.ts` (API `makeItWorkMultiTeamTrade`).
- **Starting protections:** in AI offers to you and random AI-AI trades, a 1st round pick the AI asks for starts with a protection from the AI menu 35% of the time (`withStartingProtections`, `STARTING_PROTECTION_PROB`), so the AI then asks to loosen it or keeps it (the offer text says "your 2027 1st round pick (top 5 protected)").
- **Valuation of what's owed:** the rollover / 2nd round pick owed instead of a protected pick is weighted by the chance it is delivered: 0 if the original team no longer owns it (or it is in the same trade, or already has a condition), 90% if it owns it (it could still trade it later), 90% if it isn't generated yet. Swap rights are discounted 10% for lapse risk (and worth nothing if the swapping team's own pick moves in the same trade); a pick carrying someone else's swap right is valued 90% swapped, 10% as itself. `getFallbackDeliveredProb`, `CONDITION_SURVIVE_PROB` in `ValueChangeCalculator.ts`.
- **Storage:** `draftPicks[].protection = { tid, top, rollover, then }` (tid = team that keeps it if protected) and `draftPicks[].swap = { tid }` (team holding the right), in the league file too (JSON schema updated, no DB migration needed). Pending conditions in a trade are `trade.teams[i].pickConditions`. Code: `common/draftPickConditions.ts` (text/validation), `worker/core/draft/pickConditions.ts` (resolution, called from `genOrder`), `processTrade` (apply/undo), `ValueChangeCalculator` (valuation), `trade/tryProtections.ts` (AI counters).
- **Limits:** one condition per pick; conditions can't be added to a pick that already has one, or once the draft order is set; swap rights themselves can't be re-traded (the pick carrying them can, and the right stays with the team); no "least favorable of" picks.

## Draft and scouting

### Draft Scouting

- **Where:** _Players → Draft → Draft Scouting_ (`/l/:lid/draft_scouting`).
- **College and overseas stats** (_College & Overseas Stats_): the _College stats_ button above each draft class generates college (or overseas pro club) stats and honors for its prospects from a seed (same seed, same result), or regenerates/removes them. They show on the player page with a _College_ or _Overseas_ tag in the stats tables and an _Earlier honors_ box, and are **not** pro stats: they don't count toward experience, career totals, awards, Hall of Fame or player value. The option _Generate Pre-Draft Stats Automatically_ does it for every new draft class (all classes at league creation, each season's new class, fantasy/expansion/custom classes, and imported prospects who have none). _All draft classes_ panel: the same for every upcoming class at once (seed, only prospects without stats or replace everyone's). Class year and career shape follow draft stock (`getClassIndexForStock` in `collegeStats/generate.ts`): age at the draft spans two class years, so a high-stock prospect is a year younger in school than his age says (a 20-year-old lottery pick is an older one-and-done, a 21-year-old one a sophomore), and four-year seniors are mostly lower-ranked prospects. A college season he came back after is held below declare-worthy stock (`STAY_CAP`: class z above 0.55 compressed, the excess becoming extra rating lag), so stats grow across his seasons instead of a dominant freshman staying four years. Usage has diminishing returns above 29% (max 34%) and assists per 40 cap at 9.5; see _College stats calibration_ below. Stored classes are not rewritten when the model changes, except through `COLLEGE_STATS_VERSION` (see _College stats round 2_ below), which remakes stale undrafted prospects once when a league is opened. The draft stock timeline's _Now_ point ranks by true value (the order the AI drafts in, as the Mock Draft does) rather than the fuzzed scouting list, and its random walk tightens toward the draft.
- **Regenerate ratings** (_Ratings Regenerator_): rebuilds prospects into specialized builds or archetypes (Floor General, Stretch Big, Rim Protector, …) without changing overall, potential or height. Scope: whole class, selected prospects, one prospect, or all classes at once (a preview summary per class, one _Undo last apply_ for all of them, and _Restore originals_). Settings include seed, variation, class flavor and strength, builds per class, archetype diversity, specialization and rookie caps, each lockable; per-player reroll and rating locks; preview with diffs; _Apply_, _Undo last apply_ (until reload) and _Restore originals_ (survives reloads). _Save as league default_ keeps the settings for _Re-randomize New Draft Classes_ (option; ratings first, then pre-draft stats; seeded from the league and class year; drafted players are never touched).
- **Class shape** (_Draft Class Shapes_): under each class's year, its shape, a description and 2-4 storylines read off the class (see below).
- **Scouting system** (_Scouting System_): your scouting budget gives you scouting points every season to spend on individual prospects: assign a scout (1 point) or attend a workout (3 points) in the _Scout report_ column. Each narrows how far off your view of that prospect's ratings can be and gives a scout report with ovr and pot ranges and a confidence (the _Scout report_ column of Draft Scouting and the Draft Board). Its notes are only on the prospect's own pages (College/Overseas page, _Your scouts_), not on the Draft Board or as a Draft Scouting hover. Prospects you don't scout keep the normal rating fuzz.
- **Columns:** _Archetype_ (with the Archetypes switch), _Traits_ (with player traits), badges.

### Draft class shapes

- **Where:** League Extras → _Draft Class Shapes_ (`featureDraftClassShapes`, on for new leagues, off for existing ones). Shown under each class on Draft Scouting and above the Mock Draft.
- **What:** every draft class the game generates from scratch draws a shape from the league and class year, which reshapes the top of the class (the 60 best prospects by value) within realistic bounds. 28 shapes (share of classes in brackets; _A typical class_ is the most common by far):
  - _A typical class_ (14%, no change); _A deep class_ (4%: prospects 6-45 about 2.2 ovr better); _A top-heavy class_ (3.5%: two or three franchise talents 5 better, prospects up to 35th 2 worse); _A weak class_ (4%: the top 40 2.5 worse); _A generational talent_ (1.4%: the No. 1 prospect 6.5 better, the next 29 slightly worse); _No clear No. 1_ (3.5%: the top five pulled halfway to the sixth, the next ten a little better); _Two franchise prospects_ (1.4%: the top two lifted to 4 above the better of them, prospects 3-35 0.9 worse).
  - Positions and builds (same ovr: the ratings are moved toward the build, then shifted back to the old ovr): _A guard-heavy class_ / _A big man class_ (2.8% each: half of the other prospects rebuilt toward a guard or a big, height and weight changed), _A wing class_ (2.8%: 60% of the guards and bigs toward the wing), _A point guard class_ (2.1%: 30% of the non-guards made guards, 70% of the guards made playmakers: passing, handle, IQ, a little shorter), _A stretch-big class_ (2.1%: 35% of the others made bigs, 70% of the bigs given a jumper), _A two-way wing class_ (2.1%: guards and bigs toward the wing, wings given defense and a three), _A shooters' class_ (2.8%: 55% given shooting for inside game and strength), _A defensive class_ (2.1%: 55% given diq and strength for scoring), _Athletic freaks_ (2.8%: 55% given speed, leaping and endurance for skill, half of them a year younger and 1.5 rawer), _Polished, low ceilings_ (2.1%: 60% given IQ, passing and free throws for athleticism, half of those a year older, the pool 0.6 ovr better).
  - Ages and origins: _A college-senior class_ (2.1%: 40% made seniors at the oldest draft age, 1 ovr more polish), _A high-upside class_ (2.8%: most a year younger, 1.5 ovr rawer), _A ready-now class_ (2.8%: a year older, 1.5 ovr more ready), _A one-and-done class_ (2.8%: 70% made the youngest draft age, 1.4 ovr rawer a year), _A class of late bloomers_ (2.1%: 35% a year older and 4.5 better, the pool 0.9 worse), _A boom-or-bust class_ (2.1%: neighbors on the board in pairs, one 3-6 better and the other as much worse; half a year younger and rawer), _An international class_ (2.8%: 30% of the US-born prospects from overseas instead, a name from that country and a blank college so pre-draft stats are at a pro club), _International bigs_ (1.4%: 35% from overseas, most of them rebuilt as bigs), _A home-country spike_ (1.4%: 20% of the top 45 from one country drawn from twelve; Canadians keep their US college), _Mid-major gems_ (2.1%: 45% of the US prospects in the top 45 moved to schools outside the power conferences, the top 8 a little worse and 15-45 a little better), _An injury-riddled class_ (2.1%: 30% of the top 40 come in off an injury, 1.7 ovr worse for now, the rest 0.5 better; see flavor).
  - Ages stay within the league's draft ages. **Expanded ratings** (when on): every build tilts the expanded ratings consistently: wingspan follows height changes, plus a wingspan tilt (defenders +3, athletes +2, two-way wings +3); splits move around the new diq, reb and fg + tp (shooters and stretch bigs toward catch-and-shoot, playmakers toward off the dribble; athletes toward the offensive glass, stretch bigs toward the defensive; two-way wings toward perimeter defense; guard/big nudges as before), ball security keeps its residual plus a tilt (playmakers +5, polished +4), and ovr is shifted back after (twice, because bsc is part of ovr). The test checks csh + dsh = fg + tp, reb and diq the averages of their splits, and the tilts' direction.
- **When:** a class made from scratch (each season's new class, the top-up to the number of draft classes, every class of a random players league at creation) is marked `p.draftClassShape = "pending"` when it is generated; `processNewDraftClasses` gives it its shape before anything else (so _Re-randomize New Draft Classes_ rebuilds the shaped ratings, which keeps the shaped ovr, and pre-draft stats are made from them), once: the mark becomes the shape's key. With the setting off the mark is just removed. Imported, uploaded, topped-off, real-player and scrub classes are never marked, so never reshaped.
- **Small guards (arch13):** with the Sim-Calibrated Ovr on (a new-league default) small guards lose 2-3 ovr, and BBGM's prospect generator was tuned to the stock formula, so too few made the first round. Audit (30 generated classes, full new-league pipeline: shapes, expanded ratings, ovrV2, improved positions; top 60 by value, as the AI drafts) against real drafts 2010-2025 (players' listed heights: 13.6% of first-rounders and about 16% of the top 60 at 6'3" or shorter, about 3 listed PGs and 3 Gs a first round): before, 11.8% of the first round and 15.5% of the top 60 at 6'3" or shorter, 3.3 PGs and 1.1 Gs a first round (PG or G 14.8%). Fix (`genPlayersWithoutSaving.ts`, `smallGuardPolish`): with ovrV2 on, a prospect 6'3" or shorter is generated with `SMALL_GUARD_SKILL` 6 more handle, passing and offensive IQ (3 at 6'3"; ball security follows with expanded ratings): a small player who makes the draft has made it on skill, and these are the skills a point guard is built on. After (positions with arch13's ratings-only PG bias): 17.6% of the first round and 17.8% of the top 60 at 6'3" or shorter, 12.3% at 6'2" or shorter, 4.4 PGs a first round, PG or G 21.4%; small prospects in the top 60 ovr 33.4 / pot 56.4 (others 34.4 / 56.7; before 32.3 / 54.6). The AI's draft valuation is unchanged (value plus the small need adjustment), so they are drafted where their value puts them. Class-shape and draft tests unchanged. SG generation (sgfix, Improved positions) adds a scorer guard type and tones down the smallest point guards: 17.3% of the top 60 at 6'3" or shorter (before 17.6-18.5% in the same audit).
- **Balance:** over how often each shape comes up, the top 60 of a class is as good as before (test over 10 generated classes per shape, both with expanded ratings off and on: weighted ovr change about 0.0-0.1 (limit 0.35), pot about -0.25 to -0.3 (limit 0.75); per shape within 3.5 ovr and 4.5 pot; largest per-shape moves: ready-now +1.7 ovr, weak -1.5 ovr, college-senior -2.9 pot, late bloomers -1.4 pot, one-and-done +1.2 pot). The `ratingInflation` regression test runs with shapes on (a new-league default).
- **Flavor:** under the shape's name, a description (three wordings per shape, picked from the class's seed) and 2-4 **storylines** read off the class as it is now (`common/draftClassStory.ts`, pure): always its No. 1 ("The consensus No. 1, X, is a 19-year-old center from Serbia."), then the most interesting of: how it compares with earlier classes (_The best class at the top since 2031, by the scouts' ratings_: average (ovr + pot) / 2 of the top 10; also the deepest point guard class, the youngest lottery and the most international top 30 _since_ a year or _in at least N years_; only for this year's class before its draft, compared with the last 15 drafts at their draft-year ratings, and only where at least 4 earlier classes were beaten in a row), two co-headliners / a clear No. 1 / a top three separated by a hair (by value), three+ centers or no big man in the top 10, six+ guards in the top 10, point guards in the top 30, teenagers in the top 14, 22-year-olds in the top 14, three+ of the top 30 from one country, prospects from outside North America, one school with two of the top 10, top 30 prospects from mid-majors, prospects coming back from injuries, and the college season tie-in (below). Every count in a storyline is the count in the class (a test checks every storyline of 300 random classes against the data). On Draft Scouting (each class) and the Mock Draft (shared `ui/components/DraftClassShape.tsx`).
- **News:** at the draft lottery (start of the _before draft_ phase), a headline for this year's class in the news (type Draft Lottery): "The verdict on the 2031 draft class: a guard-heavy class (the best since 2024)." plus its first two storylines. Only with the setting on and a shaped class.
- **College tie-in:** with College & Overseas Stats on too, the class's college season (version 4) matches the class 60% of the time when the shape has a match: one-and-done and high-upside → _the year of the freshman_, college-senior / late bloomers / ready-now / polished → _a veterans' year_, guard-heavy / point guard → _a guards' game_, big man → _the year of the big man_, shooters / stretch bigs → _pace and space_, mid-major gems → _the year of the mid-major_, generational → _a dominant favorite_, two franchise prospects → _two juggernauts_, no clear No. 1 → _a wide-open year_, defensive → _a defensive slog_. The season's summary then says the draft class followed it, and the class gets a storyline ("College basketball saw it coming: the season was the year of the freshman."). The injury-riddled class's marked prospects (`p.draftClassShapeInjury`) get the _injury_ college story 85% of the time (own random stream, so no one else's story changes), and with Player Traits on their durability seed is 0.6 lower.
- **Versions:** `p.draftClassShapeV` (2 now; missing = 1). Version 1 classes keep their shape and their original description word for word; the storylines are computed from the class for all shaped classes. The college tie is stored with the season (`p.collegeSeason.cs`, version 4 only), so stored seasons replay exactly.
- **Engine:** `common/draftClassShape.ts` (shapes, wordings, draw, plan, builds; pure), `common/draftClassStory.ts` (storylines, comparisons, headline; pure), `worker/core/draft/classShape.ts` (applies a plan: shifts the skill ratings to the new ovr, builds, expanded tilts, ages, origins, schools, injuries; `getClassShapeInfo` for the views, `getClassHistory`, `logClassHeadline`), tests in `worker/core/draft/classShape.test.ts`, `common/draftClassStory.test.ts`, `common/collegeStats/ncaaClassTie.test.ts`.

### College season and March Madness

- **Where:** _Draft Scouting → College/Overseas_ tab (`/l/:lid/college_overseas/:season/:tab`, tabs `standings`, `ap`, `conference`, `selection`, `ncaa`, `nit`, `overseas`), the player page (a _{year} {school}_ box next to _Earlier honors_), Ctrl+K "College/Overseas".
- **What:** a light college season for each draft class's draft year, made together with the class's college stats (the _College stats_ button, the _All draft classes_ panel, and _Generate Pre-Draft Stats Automatically_). Every school in the college list gets a team strength (conference strength + program prestige + the class's prospects on its roster, best one counting most), plays 12 non-conference games and a conference schedule (double round robin, or single round robin plus extra games to 20 for big conferences), game by game from the two strengths (scores and overtimes, no GameSim). Then conference standings, conference tournaments (everyone, top seeds get byes; Ivy top 4; winner gets the automatic bid), the final AP Top 25 (62 voters, before the NCAA tournament), Selection Sunday (32 automatic bids + 36 at-large by the committee score, see below; the last four at-large and four lowest automatic bids play the First Four for two 11 and two 16 seeds; S-curve into East/West/South/Midwest), the full 68-team bracket with scores, Final Four and champion.
- **Prospects:** a prospect at an NCAA school plays his team's games: his draft-year college row has the team's game count with the conference and NCAA tournament games at the end (always played), so totals include them. His player page shows the record, conference finish, AP rank, conference tournament result, _NCAA Tournament: Elite Eight (seed 3), South Region_ and his postseason game log. Honors (in _Earlier honors_): conference regular season / tournament champion, conference tournament MVP and All-Tournament team, NCAA Champion, Final Four Most Outstanding Player, All-Final Four team, regional Most Outstanding Player (when the best player was a prospect).
- **Page:** season selector (draft year: upcoming classes and the last 3 drafts), _Standings_ by conference, _AP Top 25_ (with prospect All-Americans), _Conference Tournaments_ (bracket per conference), _March Madness_ (champion and MOP, Final Four, one region at a time, First Four; columns scroll sideways on a phone), _Overseas_ (the class's prospects at pro clubs abroad or in the G League with league, per game stats and honors). Prospect names link to player pages and are shown under their team in brackets and tables.
- **Weekly AP poll** (_AP Top 25_ tab): 19 polls, preseason (reputation: program, prospects, a look at practice, some hype), one after each of the 17 regular season weeks (non-conference games in weeks 1-6, conference play in weeks 7-17) and the final one. The same 62 voters all year, each with a lasting opinion of every team; a team that lost all its games in a week is not moved up for it, one that won them all is not moved down. The page shows each poll with the move from the previous one, first-place votes, others receiving votes, teams that dropped out, the week's biggest risers and fallers, weeks at No. 1, and a grid of every ranked team's rank in every poll. Engine: `common/collegeStats/ncaaPolls.ts` (own random stream).
- **NET and the bubble** (_Selection_ tab): a NET-style ranking computed from results on Selection Sunday (capped margin and wins, both adjusted for schedule and site), strength of schedule rank, records by quadrant (Quadrant 1 = home vs NET top 30, neutral top 50, away top 75...) and road record. The committee score that picks and seeds the at-large teams reads SRS, the NET, the record, Quadrant 1/2 wins, Quadrant 3/4 losses and road wins. The tab lists the last four byes, last four in (the First Four at-large teams), first four out, next four out, notable snubs with the reason (AP ranked, NET top 40, regular season champion, 26 wins...) and bid thieves (conference tournament winners that would not have made it, from a league that also got an at-large team). Engine: `common/collegeStats/ncaaSelection.ts`.
- **NIT** (_NIT_ tab): the 32 best teams left out; regular season conference champions that lost their conference tournament and missed the NCAA field get automatic bids.
