Four brackets of eight (seeds 1-8), better seed at home until the semifinals, full bracket and champion. Prospects on NIT teams play the games: they are in the team's record, at the end of his draft-year stat line, in his postseason game log and a _NIT_ split, his page shows _NIT: Semifinals (seed 3)_, and honors are _NIT Champion_, _NIT Most Outstanding Player_ and _NIT All-Tournament Team_.
- **Season storylines:** each class's season draws a shape from its seed, and maybe a scoring environment, which bend what the season is simulated with. Version 3 (classes made now) draws from 20 shapes; version 2 classes keep the first seven with their old weights (a test pins whole version 2 seasons against hashes). The original seven: _a balanced year_, _a dominant favorite_ (the best team pulls 3+ points clear), _a wide-open year_ (the top of the country squeezed together, more swing in a game), _the year of the mid-major_ (non-power leagues better, six of their best teams much better), _a down year for the blue bloods_ (half of the 14 biggest programs 4-7 points worse), _one loaded conference_ (every team of one power league better), _chalk all the way_ (less swing in a game); _a scoring explosion_ or _a defensive slog_ (8 points a game more or fewer). A box at the top of the page has the headline and a written summary (whether the shape held, champion and Final Four, preseason No. 1 and weeks on top, the Cinderella, a 16 over a 1, unbeaten teams, bids by conference, first teams out, bid thieves, NIT champion, scoring). Version 3 adds: _two juggernauts_ (the two best teams 3+ points clear of the third: did they meet in the final?), _the year of the freshman_ / _a veterans' year_ / _a guards' game_ / _the year of the big man_ (prospects of that kind, by age in the draft year or position, add 35-45% more to their teams and the others 15-20% less; guards' years score 3 more points, big men's 3 fewer), _a Cinderella run_ (once the field is set, one 10-12 seed, from outside the power leagues when there is one, plays 10 points better in the tournament), _a wild first weekend_ (30% more swing in a game in the First Four and the first two rounds), _parity in the power conferences_ (power-league teams squeezed 40% toward their average), _a fallen powerhouse_ (one of the six biggest programs 8-11 points worse), _a conference down year_ (every team of one power league 3 worse), _the transfer carousel_ (every team ±2.6 points that the preseason poll does not know about), _a great team stumbles_ (the best team 4.5+ clear, 4.5 worse from the Elite Eight on), _pace and space_ (6 more points a game, more swing; never with a defensive slog). Each has its own summary sentence (two wordings for most, picked from the season; version 4 seasons pick from one or two more per shape and for the champion line, and a season that matched its draft class says so, see Draft class shapes). Engine: `common/collegeStats/ncaaStoryline.ts`. Long run over 2500 version 3 seasons: 1 seeds win the title 58% of the time (36% in wide-open years, 41% with parity in the power conferences, 52% with a Cinderella run, 58% in a balanced one, 66-68% with a dominant favorite, two juggernauts or chalk), a 16 beats a 1 in 0.9% of those games (2.7-2.8% in parity and conference-down years, 1.6% on a wild first weekend, none in chalk or freshman years), a team is unbeaten on Selection Sunday in 9% of seasons (17% with a fallen powerhouse, 15% when a great team stumbles, 3-4% in pace-and-space, big men's and guards' years), 1.35 double-digit seeds reach the Sweet 16 a year (2.1 with power-conference parity, 1.6-1.7 with a Cinderella run, a wild first weekend or pace and space; the Cinderella run's own team gets there about a third of the time), points a game 71.0 (74.7 pace and space, 72.1 guards, 69.5 big men). The test `ncaaExtras.test.ts` checks the long run over 640 seasons.
- **Versions:** `p.collegeSeason.v` is the version of the simulation the class was made with (4 now; missing = 1). Version 4 plays exactly like version 3 (a test checks whole seasons) except that a season can match its draft class's shape (`p.collegeSeason.cs`), and its summary has more wordings; version 3 seasons keep their text. Version 3 classes also store the prospect's position (`p.collegeSeason.pos`), so a guards' or big men's year is rebuilt the same way after his ratings change. A version 1 class (made before the weekly polls, the NIT and the storylines) is rebuilt playing every game exactly as it was played then, so its champion, records, final AP poll, bracket and prospects' lines do not change (a test pins this against hashes of the old output). It still gets the weekly polls, the NET, the bubble (in the order its field was really picked: SRS and record), a written summary and an NIT bracket, but its NIT games are not counted in records or prospects' stats (the tab says so), and its shape is always _a balanced year_. Prospects added later to such a class (only missing) keep it version 1.
- **Cache:** the rebuilt season is kept per class (last 8, keyed by league, draft year, seed, version and every prospect's school and boost), so the page and player pages do not replay it on every load (about 40 ms to build).
- **Storage:** no new store. Each prospect at an NCAA school gets `p.collegeSeason = { season, seed, school, boost, summary, v }`; the season is rebuilt exactly from the class's seed and every prospect's school and boost (`getCollegeSeason` in `worker/core/draft/collegeSeason.ts`), deterministic per seed. Generating stats for only the prospects missing them keeps the class's existing season (newcomers join with boost 0). Removing a class's college stats removes it. Engine: `common/collegeStats/ncaa.ts` (tests in `ncaa.test.ts`), stats integration in `common/collegeStats/generate.ts` (`ncaaSeason` option) and `season.ts` (`teamGames`/`postseasonGames`).

### College stats calibration (colstats round)

Audit of the draft-year college line of generated classes (6 classes of real BBGM prospects, expanded ratings off and on, NCAA schools with 40 minute games, 10+ games) against Division I numbers, and the fixes in `common/collegeStats/season.ts`:

- **Why it looked inflated:** every prospect was a 30 minute starter (base 32 mpg, p10 25 mpg, only talent moved it by 2.7 per class SD), so the bottom of a class scored 11 ppg; per-40 rates were already about right. Now a class-average prospect is a ~22 minute rotation player and minutes come from role (4.3 per class SD of talent, freshmen -2.6 / seniors +1.6, endurance), capped at 36.5.
- **Strengths and weaknesses:** the 3PA share is a smooth function of tp (none at tp 22 or less, the height's share at about 37, up to 1.7x for snipers, plus a little for stretch bigs) instead of a step at tp 30 that let non-shooters take a third of their shots from three; 3P% has a steeper tp slope (and leans on csh with expanded ratings, capped at 47%); FT rate reads dunk/inside aggression; rebounds read hgt, reb, jumping and wingspan (bigs 10-13 per 40); steals read perimeter D (diq) more than speed and come up to D-I levels (~1.3 per 40); blocks read jumping, interior D (diq) and wingspan more; ball security moves turnovers more; usage leans more on the scoring composite than on overall talent; the efficiency premium is a little smaller and the rim FG% cap is 78%.
- **No change elsewhere:** the stats use their own seeded streams, so nothing about draft class generation, the game sim or ratings changes; stored seasons are not rewritten. Two story hooks in `career.ts` were retuned for the new minutes (a bust's freshman minutes boost 2.5 → 4).
- **Tests:** `collegeStats/calibration.test.ts` (level and spread, shooting splits, size-based rebounding/blocks, rating correlations, expanded rating effects, freshmen vs seniors); `generate.test.ts` average mpg range 24-33 → 19-27.

### College stats round 2 (colstats2): what a new league actually shows

The colstats audit only looked at the current draft class. A new league has seven (`numDraftClasses`), and every class made after that is made seven years ahead, so nearly every class a user drafts from had its stats generated years before its draft year.

- **Root cause:** `toCollegeProspect` projected the ratings of a class years away forward (`forwardProject`: about +4 a year at 18, +3 at 19, +2.4 at 20, so +10 to +25 on every skill for classes 3-6 years out), assuming prospects develop before the draft. They don't: `newPhasePreseason` develops only players with tid ≥ FREE_AGENT, so an undrafted prospect's ratings now are his ratings in his draft year. The class z (minutes, usage) is relative and barely moved, but everything read from absolute ratings did: the 3PA share saturated for everyone (non-shooters with tp 25-29 projected to 45+ took 60% of their shots from three), 3P% and FT% rose, assist rates from pss rose. So classes 3-6 years out had median 13.5-15.9 ppg, 3.9-4.7 apg and 3PA rates of 0.55 (the current class: 9 ppg, 2 apg, 0.17-0.3), and every prospect looked like a high-assist, high-volume three-point shooter. The UI showed exactly what was stored; there was no display bug.
- **Fix:** no projection (`toCollegeProspect` reads the stored ratings). Partial generation (prospects missing stats, a remake of some) measures class rank against the whole class (`classProspects` option). Ratings lean a little more on the matching stat: rebounds 0.15 hgt + 0.13 reb per point, blocks read interior D (0.045 per point) more and jumping less, bigs get fewer assists than their passing says (−2.4 per 40 at full size), a non-shooter's 3PA share no longer gets random threes (the noise scales with his willingness), usage 0.044 per SD, minutes 4.8 per class SD capped at 35.5, rim FG% capped at 75% and post-ups at 66%. The G League is a pro roster (34 mpg cap, usage −0.035, minutes −5), and Overtime Elite and prep/postgrad seasons are 32-minute games at a per-game pace.
- **Existing leagues:** generated stats carry `p.collegeStatsV` (`COLLEGE_STATS_VERSION`, now 2). When a league is opened (`beforeLeague`), `draft.upgradeCollegeStats` remakes every undrafted prospect with generated stats (a stored career) and no or an older stamp, class by class, with the class's own seed (the one stored with its college season, else the automatic seed) and the class's college season kept exactly (schedule, results, bracket, each prospect's place and boost). Generation is deterministic, so this is a one-time, repeatable change. Drafted players keep the stats they were drafted with, and imported rows without a stored career are not touched.
- **Tests:** `worker/core/draft/collegeStatsUiPath.test.ts` builds seven classes the way a new league does (preseason, auto seed, the worker's `generateCollegeStats`) and reads the lines off the stored prospects: every class on the same scale, D-I levels and spread, top-5 vs the rest, the class leader in assists/blocks/rebounds/threes/steals ranks high in pss/hgt+idef/hgt+reb/tp/pdef, non-shooters and non-shooting bigs take almost no threes, pass-first guards vs rim protectors, the version stamp, and `upgradeCollegeStats` (remakes a stale class exactly from its seed, keeps its college season, leaves drafted players alone, then has nothing left to do).

### Prospect careers: transfers, redshirts, recruiting, unusual prospects

Part of _College & Overseas Stats_ (no toggle of its own): everything here is made when a class's college stats are generated, and is biography built around the ratings a prospect already has. Nothing reads back into ratings, age, value or how draft classes are generated for the pro game.

- **Where:** the player's _College/Overseas_ page (`/l/:lid/player_pre_draft/:pid`): a story line under the cards, _Scouting report_, _Teams_ (class year, notes), _Stats_, _Recruiting_, _Draft stock_.
- **Multi-school careers** (`common/collegeStats/career.ts`): a career plan is drawn per prospect from his own stream (seed, draft year, pid), separate from his stats stream, so a prospect with a plain career gets the same seasons as before. The draft-year school is always his own (the one tied to the simulated college season); earlier years can be elsewhere.
  - _Transfers_ happen between seasons, never during one (one school per season): up-transfers (a breakout at a smaller school, then a bigger program), down-transfers (buried on a big program's bench, then real minutes) and lateral ones. About 13% of sophomores, 25% of juniors and 34% of seniors at NCAA schools have transferred; a few seniors twice.
  - _Redshirt_ (about 6-11% of multi-year players, more for lesser prospects): his first year on campus with no games (no stats row; listed in _Teams_). It takes the place of a season, since his age is fixed: a redshirt junior (_RS Jr._) is 22. A 23-year-old senior usually has a redshirt year as his fifth year.
  - _Medical redshirt_ (about 4% of juniors and seniors): a season that ended after 3-9 games and did not count against his eligibility.
  - _Junior college_ (about 4-9% of juniors and seniors, more for lesser prospects): one or two NJCAA seasons (inflated numbers against weak competition, _NJCAA All-American_ / _All-Region_ honors) before a Division I school.
  - Class years (_Fr._, _RS So._ ...) are eligibility years. Honors, coaches and team records follow the school of each season.
- **Unusual prospects:** each prospect has a small seeded chance (about 8-10% of a class end up with one) of a story that fits what he already is: _five-star bust_ (a below-average prospect in his class: a top-25 recruit and lottery projection on arrival, fewer minutes every year), _unranked riser_ (a top prospect nobody ranked: bench minutes and often a small school or junior college early), _late-blooming senior_ (barely played until his last season), _7'3"+ project_ (from his listed height: raw, limited minutes), _undersized scorer from a small school_ (6'1" or under at a low-major: carries the offense), _older international_ (22+, a pro abroad since 17 or 18, up to six seasons), _reclassified freshman_ (enrolled a year early), _injury-shortened season_ (his draft-year season ends after 5-15 games; with the college season on he misses the postseason). Minutes, usage, the draft stock timeline and the recruiting rank reflect it, and the page says it in a line.
- **Recruiting:** for prospects with a US high school path (an NCAA or junior college first stop): stars, national and position rank, composite rating (a fixed function of the rank that agrees with the stars), the number of offers and the programs worth naming, finalists, early (November) or late (April) signing, and high school all-star selections (generic names: _High School All-American Game_, _National Senior All-Star Classic_, _Prep Hoops Summit_, _State Player of the Year_). Ranks are against the rest of his high school class, which is spread over several draft classes, so no two players of a class share a national rank or a rank at their position (`worker/core/draft/recruiting.ts`, `resolveClassRanks` in `profile.ts`). The rank is set once, when his stats are generated, and stored; players ranked later fit around it. Offers, finalists and the rest are derived from the rank and the player (not stored), so they also appear for older classes.
- **Scouting report:** strengths, weaknesses and a projection (`common/collegeStats/scoutingReport.ts`), written only from what the user can already see: draft-year ratings with scouting fuzz applied (never true ratings; none at all with the hidden ratings challenge, when the report is written from production alone), the archetype label Draft Scouting shows, height, pre-draft production and where the user's board has him (or where he went). Player traits are never used. Appears for old classes too.
- **Storage:** `p.preDraftCareer = { years: [{ season, school, level, cls, status?, move? }], story?, hsClass?, recruit?: { rank, posRank } }` on prospects whose stats were generated with the career model; removed with the class's college stats. Stats rows are unchanged (one row per season played, `teamName` = that season's school). **Classes generated before this keep their stored seasons**: the page falls back to what the rows say (one class year per row, recruiting rank derived as before and only bumped to the next free rank on a collision within the high school class). A stored career that no longer matches the rows (edited or re-imported stats) is ignored.
- **Engine hooks:** `season.ts` `mpgShift`, `usageShift`, `gamesCap` (all optional; nothing changes without them). Tests: `career.test.ts`, `profile.test.ts`, `scoutingReport.test.ts`, `worker/core/draft/recruiting.test.ts`.

### Draft board and AI drafting

- **Draft Board** (_Draft Board_): `/l/:lid/draft_board[/:season]`, linked from Draft Scouting and the Draft page. Rank your prospects, give them tiers and notes next to their pre-draft stats, builds and scout reports. Option _Auto Draft From My Board_: when auto drafting for you, the top-ranked available prospect on your board is picked. The Draft page also has a _Draft-day trades & team boards_ window (trades through the trade API, each AI team's needs and board).
- **AI drafts by need** (_AI Drafts by Need_, basketball): AI picks get a small need (guard, wing, big) and strategy (contenders want players who are ready, rebuilding teams want ceiling) adjustment on top of value, at most about 2 value points (`draftNeeds.ts`; value still decides most picks). Off: the old pure-value drafting.
- **Mock draft** (_Mock Draft_, basketball): `/l/:lid/mock_draft`, a tab of Draft Scouting. A two-round mock draft of the current class (until its draft is over; during the draft, the remaining picks). Each team picks the way the AI does in `runPicks.ts`: value plus the need/strategy adjustment (when _AI Drafts by Need_ is on) and the player-traits nudge (when traits are on), with the same weighted random choice. The order is the league's draft order with traded picks once the lottery is done, otherwise one simulated lottery (like the projected Draft Lottery page). Pick protections and swaps are resolved on that order as a dry run with the same code as the real draft (`resolvePickConditionsPure` on copies: no saves, no news), and each affected pick says what happened (swapped, protected and stays, conveyed). Each pick shows why (best available, fills a need, ceiling or ready-now, traits, reach or steal against your scouting list); _Rerun_ draws again; your picks are highlighted. Pro projections: 120 careers per prospect run with the game's own development (`developSeasonSync`) from his ratings as your scouts see them, with a simple out-of-the-league rule, giving the median and 10th-90th percentile peak ovr and star (65+) / starter (58+) / bust (under 50 or gone within 4 seasons) odds, ported from the Draft Workshop. Cached per league, season and ratings in the worker; shown on the Mock Draft tab and as a _Pro projection_ section on a prospect's player page (`mockDraft.ts`, `proProjection.ts`, `mockDraftData.ts`).

### Draft lottery night and combine

- **Where:** League Extras → _Draft Lottery Night & Combine_ (`featureLotteryNight`, basketball, on for new leagues, off for existing ones; hidden in college leagues). Off: the Draft Lottery page, the College/Overseas combine, scouting reports and the AI draft are exactly as before, and nothing below runs.
- **Lottery night** (Draft Lottery page, `/l/:lid/draft_lottery`): _Start lottery night_ runs the real lottery once, in the worker, exactly as _Start lottery_ does without the setting (same code, same random numbers, same result: `draftCombine.test.ts` checks the picks and the number of draws with the setting on and off). The reveal is purely presentational: picks come out from the last lottery pick up to No. 1, every 2.5 seconds or with _Reveal next_, with _Pause_ and _Reveal all_. Each pick gets a stage card in the team's colors with its logo, "Moved up from 7th!" / "Fell from 2nd" / "Stayed at 5th", its odds of that pick going in and a one-line reaction (the AI GM by name and leanings with AI GM personalities on, otherwise the fans by the team's hype; your own team gets its own lines; the wording is chosen by a hash of season and team, no random numbers). Teams still in the running are listed with their No. 1 odds, and "Down to the final two" before the last reveal. Then a _Lottery winners and losers_ recap (biggest jumps and falls, the longest shot) that stays on the page for past lotteries, and a news item with the same summary (no notification, so it doesn't spoil the reveal). The pick table and its highlighting below work as before. Code: `common/lotteryNight.ts`, `ui/views/DraftLotteryNight.tsx`, the reveal state in `ui/views/DraftLottery.tsx`, the news in `genOrder` → `logLotteryNightNews`.
- **Draft combine:** held at the start of the draft lottery phase (after the playoffs) for the current class, with a news item (number measured, how many skipped the drills, the best vertical, lane agility and wingspan). About 1.25 prospects per draft pick are invited (75 for 60 picks), by projected stock from their draft-year ratings. Everyone invited is measured: height with and without shoes, wingspan, standing reach, weight, body fat, hand length and width. The drills: lane agility, shuttle, 3/4 court sprint, standing and max vertical, bench press reps (185 lbs), and shooting drills (spot-up threes out of 25, off-dribble jumpers out of 25, free throws out of 20). Results come from his draft-year ratings (hgt and wng with expanded ratings, stre, spd, jmp, endu, tp/ft/fg or catch-and-shoot/off-the-dribble with expanded ratings) and listed height and weight, plus realistic noise from a small RNG seeded by pid and draft year (`common/draftCombine.ts`): the same every time, for old classes too, and the game's random numbers are never used. **Stored when held:** each invited prospect's results are saved on him (`p.combine`, with his season) when the combine is held, so regenerating his ratings (Ratings Regenerator, a new build) or other later changes don't rewrite the combine; a class held before this (no stored results) is still computed live. Some skip the drills: top-3 prospects half the time, top 8 30%, injured prospects 85%, everyone else rarely; they are still measured.
- **Scaled to real combines** (2000s-2020s). Generated classes (8 classes, 560 prospects, `draftCombine.test.ts`), mean ± sd vs real: height without shoes 77.6 ± 3.7" (real 77.6 ± 3.3), wingspan 81.3 ± 4.3" (81.8 ± 3.9), standing reach 104.1 ± 5.1" (103.5 ± 5), body fat 7.3 ± 2.3% (7.2 ± 2.6), hand length 8.74 ± 0.51" (8.75 ± 0.45), hand width 9.49 ± 0.73" (9.5 ± 0.75), lane agility 11.38 ± 0.43 s (11.3 ± 0.45), shuttle 3.09 ± 0.13 s (3.08 ± 0.15), sprint 3.30 ± 0.11 s (3.28 ± 0.12), standing vertical 29.4 ± 4.2" (29.3 ± 3), max vertical 34.5 ± 4.0" (34.5 ± 3.7), bench 9.0 ± 4.5 reps (9 ± 5), spot-up 52% (53%), off-dribble 43% (44%), free throws 74% (75%). The test checks every mean within half a real sd, the sd within 0.55-1.6× and the 2nd/98th percentiles inside the real extremes (± half an sd).
- **Combine tab** of Draft Scouting (`/l/:lid/draft_combine[/:season]`): a sortable table of every invited prospect (DNP for skipped drills, green/red for the top/bottom 5 of each test, an athletic score = average percentile of the agility, speed and leaping drills, and the draft pick for past classes), _Standouts_ and _Disappointments_ by athletic score with each one's best or worst test, and the top 3 per test. Measurements and drill results only: no scouting info.
- **Player page:** the prospect's College/Overseas page (`/l/:lid/player_pre_draft/:pid`) has a _Draft Combine_ section with every result, his rank and percentile among the prospects measured (or "not invited"). It replaces the College/Overseas combine (College stats setting) while the setting is on.
- **Feeds scouting** (Scouting Reports setting): for a prospect who was measured, the size/length grade's range shrinks to 35% and his scout's noise on it to 40%; for one who did the drills, the same for athleticism (range 50%). The physical profile gets a _Combine_ line ("Measured 6'8" without shoes, 7'1.5" wingspan (+5.5" ape index). Top-2 max vertical (40.5"), best lane agility (10.4s)", "Skipped the drills while injured"), and the athleticism and size grade lines read the combine's vertical and wingspan. Still only on player pages.
- **AI draft:** each tested prospect has a _performance_: his drill results against what his ratings predict, about N(0, 1). Only big over/under-performers (beyond ±1) get a nudge of up to 0.75 value points (0.6 per sd beyond 1), then the nudge is centered so it averages exactly zero over the class (uninvited prospects and skippers included). AI teams add it in `runPicks` like the other draft adjustments; the mock draft uses it too ("Combine riser"). Your own picks never get it.
- **Tests:** `src/common/draftCombine.test.ts` (same results per pid and season, no `Math.random`, skip rates, ranks, the nudge summing to zero and bounded, reveal order, jumps/falls/recap, deterministic reactions, narrower grade ranges), `src/worker/core/draftCombine/draftCombine.test.ts` (generated classes against real ranges, the nudge over a class, the lottery with the setting on and off).

### Scouting reports

- **Where:** League Extras → _Scouting Reports_ (`featureScoutingReports`, basketball, on for new leagues, off for existing ones; hidden in college leagues). With it off, the short report on the College/Overseas page stays exactly as it was.
- **Player page:** a _Scouting_ entry in the player dropdown (next to Overview, Game Log, College/Overseas, Traits; `/l/:lid/player_scouting/:pid[/:scoutId]`) for every player: prospects, pros, free agents and retired players, and a one-line summary on the Overview.
  - _Header:_ archetype name only, comparisons ("Plays like X (2031 draft, No. 4; became a starter)", the closest past players of this league at the draft for prospects, at the same age for pros), floor and ceiling roles ("Floor: Rotation wing · Ceiling: All-Star"), and a confidence from the remaining scouting uncertainty (looks, budget) and the lead scout.
  - _Grades:_ letter grades A+ ... F for shooting (catch-and-shoot, off the dribble, free throws), finishing, playmaking, ball security, rebounding, perimeter defense, interior defense, athleticism, size/length, basketball IQ, motor/intangibles. Each is the percentile of a 0-100 score among his peers (the draft class's guards/wings/bigs for prospects, the league's for pros, today's league at his peak for retired players; the reference is stated), with a one-line explanation (several phrasings per level, plus the stat that backs it up). When unsure the grade is a range ("B to A-").
  - _Breakdowns:_ offense (scorer type, shot mix from his shot locations, an estimated self-created share, usage), defense (who he can guard, switchability, rim protection, team defense), physical profile (measurements, frame, room to grow, combine percentiles for prospects), intangibles from trait readings (visible traits as scout observations, hidden traits "Unknown: needs more looks" until they are known), red/green flags (injury history with body parts, FT vs 3P mismatch, turnovers, age vs production, competition level, tools vs production, decline), development plan (typical league aging only, never his own curve; work ethic only once known; the head coach's development philosophy and the development assistant with Coaching Staff/Systems), team fit (needs filled, redundancy, coaching system fit), a pro report (role, trend, ideal role, contract value, how to use him) and a bottom line with a draft range or role/contract recommendation.
  - _Printable card_ (Print scouting card: grade bars, radar of key grades, shot-mix chart, comparison cards, bottom line; prints on one letter page with only the card, in light colors in either theme, two columns, colors kept; the newspaper's print rule is scoped to the newspaper page, it used to blank every other printout), _two scouts' takes_ side by side, _stock up / stock down_ changelog ("Raised: Catch-and-shoot B- → B after shooting 41.0% from three"), and the _track record_ ("Our scouts said: lottery talent → became an All-Star").
- **Scouting department** (`/l/:lid/scouting_department[/:team]`, a _Scouts_ tab of Draft Scouting): every team has 2-5 scouts from its scouting budget level (three-season average), with names, faces (in suits), a specialty (college, international, pro, athletes, shooters), a quality and a small bias (overrates athletes, falls for shooters, loves size, trusts the box score, tools over production, tough/generous grader, young upside). The best-matching scout writes a report, the next one gives the second opinion; any scout can be picked. Better departments mean narrower ranges, earlier red flags (injury and shooting flags need less evidence) and better comparisons. Scouts are hired and leave on their own (each slot is a chain of scouts staying 2-10 years, drawn from the league seed), with hit rates per scout once their draft calls can be judged (4 seasons after the draft, or retirement).
- **PDF export:** the _Download player_ PDF has the report as its own page (grade bars, radar, shot mix, comparisons, flags, bottom line, pro report, offense, defense, physical profile, intangibles, development, team fit), continuing on more pages when it doesn't fit; _Export profile_ adds the two scouts' takes, stock up/down and the track record.
- **Only on player pages:** scouting info (grades, blurbs, reports) is shown only on the player's own pages (the Scouting page, the Overview summary, the pre-draft view) and in the player PDF export. The Mock Draft/Draft Board hover, the trade window and free agent blurbs, the Playoffs opponent scouting and the Newspaper draft preview were removed (hotfix); the components are kept for reuse. AI GMs still read grades through their analytics/old-school lens on draft day (weights sum to zero over grades that are z-scores within the class, at most 0.8 value points, so no inflation).
- **For other features:** `worker/core/scoutingReports` exports `getScoutingSplit(pid)` / `getScoutingRumors(limit)` ("Scouts are split on X: one sees an All-Star, another a rotation wing") used by the rumor mill's _Scouting buzz_ (see Rumors); the scouting reports don't depend on the rumors feature.
- **Hidden information:** reports read only ratings with the user's scouting fuzz (none under the no visible ratings challenge, then production only with wide ranges), trait readings as the Traits page shows them (an unknown hidden trait carries no number), stats, measurements and public records. Never true prospect ratings, hidden trait scores or the aging curve (tests check the inputs against the true values).
- **Storage:** reports are derived on view. Stored on players: `p.scoutSnaps` (up to 8 snapshots: season, phase, one character per grade, overall, looks, five rounded stats; taken in the preseason, at the trade deadline, at the end of the regular season and before the draft, for players and this/next year's prospects) and `p.scoutDraft` (the department's take at the draft: tier, scout ids, each scout's tier, overall grade), under 200 bytes per player. Old leagues work when it's turned on; their history starts then.
- **Code:** `common/scoutingReports/` (pure: grades, categories, scouts, report, history, blurbs, tests), `worker/core/scoutingReports/` (inputs, pages, snapshots via a hook in `newPhase`, rumors, newspaper facts, GM lens in `runPicks`), `worker/api/scouting.ts`, `ui/components/ScoutingReport/`, `ui/views/PlayerScouting.tsx`, `ui/views/ScoutingDepartment.tsx`, `common/newspaper/pages-scouting.ts`.

### Customize Player: Regenerate build

- **Where:** a player page → _Edit_ (`/l/:lid/customize_player/:pid`), _Regenerate build_ button (opens a dialog).
- **What:** the same build engine for one existing player: preview, pin ratings, reroll; keeps ovr, pot and height.

## College mode

- **Where:** New League → College (`/new_league/college`, dashboard button, Ctrl+K "New League > College"). In a college league: League → _Top 25 & Selection_ (`college_polls`), _Conf. Tournaments_ (`college_conf_tournaments`); Players → _Recruiting_ (`college_recruiting`), _Departures & Arrivals_ (`college_departures`).
- **What:** play as a college program. Schools from the prospect model's school list (three sizes: power conferences 79 schools, major conferences 145, all of Division I 364), college rules (two 20-minute halves, 30-second shot clock, 5 fouls, one-and-one from the 7th team foul, two shots from the 10th), 13 scholarships, no contracts/trades/free agency/finances (pages hidden). Season: non-conference then conference schedule, weekly AP Top 25 from the real results, conference tournaments (champions get automatic bids), Selection (NET, quadrants, committee score, S-curve), national tournament (32 or 64) with the real game sim. Offseason: seniors graduate, the best players leave in an off-screen pro draft, an automatic transfer portal, recruiting (a class of high school prospects with stars and ranks; the user picks targets or leaves _Auto recruit_ on), signing day with news, walk-ons. Prestige drifts with results; the user's job security is judged against what the program's prestige predicts.
- **v2:** _College Quick Sim_ (`collegeQuickSim`, League Extras: Game Simulation; on by default for the major and full sizes): games without the user's team are played by a statistical model calibrated to the full sim (`college/quickSim.ts`), the user's games, conference tournament finals and the national tournament use the full sim; the full size sims a regular season in about 21-26 s headless instead of about 125 s. College awards (Player of the Year, All-American 1st/2nd/3rd teams, Freshman of the Year, Defensive POY, Most Outstanding Player, conference POY and all-conference teams, All-Freshman, Coach of the Year against prestige expectations) as award settings, so the awards, history and player pages show them. College-scale team rating (best programs in the high 80s, weakest in the 40s). Bracket: "National Champions", region names. Redshirts (Roster page _Redshirt_ button on freshmen who have not played; AI redshirts deep-bench freshmen). League files with players keep their rosters (class years from age) with a notice.
- **v3:** First Four (68-team field at the full size, `collegeFirstFour`), _College NIT_ (`collegeNit`, League Extras: Game Simulation; 32 or 16 teams, automatic bids for regular season champions, `/college_nit`), recruiting v2 (weekly points, scout / contact / home visit / official visit / offer / NIL with pitches, interest bars against the top 3 schools, in-season commitments and decommitments, signing day results with class rankings), a user-run transfer portal (`/college_portal`, 3 portal weeks, retention pitches, one transfer per player). Details and the interest model in [College mode](college-mode.md).
- **Rule settings for any league:** _Custom Shot Clock_ (`customShotClock`, `shotClockLength`) and _One-and-One Bonus_ (`oneAndOne`, `oneAndOneFouls`) in League Extras. Off = the sim is byte-identical (`outcomeInvariance.test.ts`).
- **Design, data model, calendar, speed and roadmap:** [docs/college-mode.md](college-mode.md). Code: `src/worker/core/college/`, `src/common/college/`, gated by `isCollege()` (`leagueType: "college"`). League Extras that need contracts, trades or a draft have `college: false` in the registry and are always off in a college league.
- **Tests:** `src/worker/core/college/collegeSeasons.test.ts` (creates a college league and plays three seasons through every phase; `COLLEGE_LEVEL`, `COLLEGE_SEASONS`, `COLLEGE_BENCH=<file>` for timings and stats), `schedule.test.ts`, `quickSim.test.ts` (calibration bounds and box score consistency); calibration scripts `collegeCalibration.script.test.ts`, `quickSimData.script.test.ts` (dumps a season of full-sim or quick-sim box scores for fitting). `COLLEGE_QUICK=1/0` forces the quick sim in `collegeSeasons.test.ts`.

## Coaching staff

- **Where:** Team menu → _Coaching Staff_ (`/l/:lid/staff`, `/l/:lid/staff/:team`), League menu → _Coaches_ (`/l/:lid/coaches[/:season]`: head coaches, the season's coaching carousel, Coach of the Year history), and a page per coach (`/l/:lid/coach/:cid`). Team Finances links to the staff instead of offering the coaching slider.
- **What:** every team has a head coach and three assistants (player development, offense, defense) with ratings (development, offense, defense, motivation), a style (pace, threes, defense, rotation, each -1 to 1), age and a contract. Salaries are paid as coaching expenses (not against the salary cap). The staff's development score is turned into an equivalent coaching budget level, so it replaces the coaching budget's effect on progs, normalized by the league's average staff each time levels are synced, so the league-wide development effect stays centered like default AI coaching budgets while the coach pool's ratings drift over the decades (see `staff.test.ts` and [rating-inflation.md](rating-inflation.md)). A team's score carries 65% of last season's (`t.staffDevCarry`), so a new coach's effect phases in and a team's development edge is stable from year to year (year-to-year change SD of the effect 2.4% → 1.1% of progs, same spread across teams; coach ratings move SD 1 a year instead of 1.5; see rating-inflation.md, "year-to-year swings"). After the playoffs each season: records are written to each coach (a coach hired or fired during the season gets only the games he was in charge of), Coach of the Year is awarded, coaches age/improve/retire, contracts expire, AI teams fire (losing head coaches especially) and hire from the pool (bigger markets pay more). Your expiring coaches leave unless you _Extend_ them (the new salary, his asking price, starts at once, but never below what he makes now); firing pays half the remaining contract as a buyout. Any vacancy still open in the preseason is filled automatically.
- **Pages** (`src/ui/views/Staff/`, `Coaches.tsx`, `Coach.tsx`; shared pieces in `src/ui/components/CoachUi.tsx`, wording and "what does this mean?" texts in `src/common/staffText.ts`): _Coaching Staff_ leads with the head coach (portrait, record against the preseason expectation, contract, overall; with Coaching Systems: both systems in plain words, scheme fit with the players helping and hurting it and the systems the roster would fit best, installation, job security, reputation, philosophy, personality, mentor), a _What the staff does for the team_ card (development level and rank, each job's share of player development, game-day strengths, payroll) and one card per assistant (what the job does, ratings as words and bars). For your team: _Hire a coach_ (one job at a time, recommendation tags for the leader in overall, fit with your roster, development, tactics and reputation, filters by system, "better than my coach", a sortable table with each candidate's difference from your coach and the development level you would have with him), a side-by-side _Compare_ with a better/worse verdict, and confirm dialogs that spell out the contract, buyout, the vacancy's effect on development and the installation period before anything happens (_Replace_ fires the current coach, then hires). _Coaches_ is a sortable table with views for head coaches, all staff and available coaches (fit with your roster), filters by team, system and job security, then the carousel as tagged moves and Coach of the Year. Each concept has a help popover. With Coaching Systems off the pages say so and hide the systems columns.
- **Staff faces:** coaches and GMs are drawn in suits (`src/common/faceParts.suits.ts`, `src/ui/components/StaffFace.tsx`): six original facesjs "jersey" parts (charcoal, navy, grey, open collar, blazer, team quarter-zip) registered with the fork's face parts but never given to players, picked per person from his id, with the team's colours on the tie or pin (a white or black first colour is skipped) and hair that greys from the mid 40s. Applied when drawn, so stored faces are unchanged and existing leagues get suits too.
- **Setting:** `coachingStaff` (basketball only). On by default for new leagues; existing leagues (DB version 76 migration) and league files from before version 76 keep the slider until it's turned on, which generates staffs.
- **Storage:** `coaches` object store (key `cid`, index `tid`), included in league export under _All team data_.
- **For other features:** `src/worker/core/staff/data.ts` has `getTeamCoachStyle(tid)` (for team strategy / game sim later), `getCoachingCarousel(season)`, `getHeadCoachForSeason(tid, season)` and `getCoachAwards(type)`.
- **Known limits:** in-game effects come with [Coaching systems](#coaching-systems) (`coachSystems`); without it the style is exposed only. Trainers are not modeled; scouts are, as a separate department (see [Scouting reports](#scouting-reports)). With the budget disabled, every team gets the average development effect, as with the slider.

## AI GM personalities

- **Where:** _Front Offices_ tab of League History (`/l/:lid/front_offices`; also in the League "More" links and the command palette), a GM card on each AI team's roster page (face, style, tenure, record, job security, _Show leanings_), and news items when a GM is hired, fired or retires. _Why?_ on the Trade page names the AI team's GM and how his leanings moved an asset's value.
- **What:** every AI team has a general manager with a name, face, age and ten fixed leanings, each from -1 to 1 (`GmTraits`, `src/common/types.gm.ts`): timeline (win-now ↔ patient), evaluation (old-school ↔ analytics), risk, luxury tax tolerance, draft pick valuation, trade frequency, loyalty, free agency (depth ↔ stars), draft board (need ↔ best available) and prospects (ready-now ↔ upside). They're drawn around 0 (sd about 0.45, a few mildly correlated: win-now GMs spend more and trade picks), so the league as a whole values players, picks and contracts like before. Your team has no GM unless the AI runs it (spectator mode or auto play); its card says _General manager: You_.
- **Effects** (`gmWeightsFromTraits` in `src/common/gmPersonality.ts`, all neutral at 0; with the setting off none of this code runs and every decision is exactly as before):
  - _Trade value_ (`team/ValueChangeCalculator.ts`): future picks and players ≤ 23 ×(1 ± 0.07) by timeline, veterans ≥ 29 the other way (±0.06); picks ×(1 ± 0.12) by pick valuation; a player style factor ±6% (analytics GMs like youth, potential, PER beyond the ratings; old-school GMs like ovr, size and experience); raw prospects ±3% by risk; the injury discount ×(1 ∓ 0.35) by risk; the weight of good/bad contracts ×(1 ∓ 0.3) by tax tolerance; and his own drafted or 3+ season players ±6% by loyalty when he'd give them up (which also drives re-signing, since that uses the same math). Trade values are value⁷, so these are smaller than the team strategy factors.
  - _Trades_ (`trade/betweenAiTeams.ts`, `trade/aiNeeds.ts`, `trade/aiOffers.ts`): each team's chance of being picked for an AI-AI trade attempt (both sides, every need-driven kind) and for an offer to you is ×(1 + 0.55 × trade frequency), averaging 1; win-now GMs are deadline buyers at a lower win% (0.45 ± 0.06); tax-tolerant GMs dump salary only a bit further over the tax (±5%).
  - _Free agency_ (`freeAgents/autoSign.ts`): dealmakers are a bit more active each day; star hunters rank expensive free agents up to 2.5 value points higher, depth builders cheaper ones. Contract amounts are unchanged (demands come from the player).
  - _Re-signing, extensions, options_ (`phase/newPhaseResignPlayers.ts`, `contract/hooks.ts`, `contract/options.ts`): the roster rank past which a team won't re-sign into the luxury tax (`AVOID_TAX_MIN_RANK`, 3) moves ±1 by tax tolerance; loyal GMs extend up to 60% more often and keep team options at a slightly lower bar; big spenders extend a top-2 player into the tax.
  - _Draft_ (`draft/runPicks.ts`, `draft/draftNeeds.ts`, mock draft): the need adjustment of _AI Drafts by Need_ ×(1 ∓ 0.75) by best available vs need, and up to ±1.5 value points for upside vs ready-now (pot - ovr gap and age).
  - _Strategy_ (`team/updateStrategies.ts`): switch thresholds shift ±8 points (win-now GMs go for it sooner and hold on longer).
  - _Coaches_ (`staff/index.ts`, with the coaching staff on): analytics/patient GMs weigh development over motivation when hiring; patient GMs fire coaches less (×(1 ∓ 0.35)).
- **Jobs:** when the regular season starts each GM gets an expectation, the Season Odds projected wins (or, with Season Odds off, the roster rating against the league average). He's judged only on full seasons he ran (`judgedSeasons`): seasons before he was hired, or one he took over after it started, don't count. After the playoffs each season scores wins above the expectation (per 82 games), where after his first season the expectation is 60% the projection and 40% an improvement path from what his inherited roster was projected to win (a bad team is expected to climb towards .500 over five seasons, seven when rebuilding; a good team to stay good; `gmPathExpWon`), plus playoff success (2 for making it, 3 per round won), minus a penalty for missing the playoffs only when he was projected at .550 or better, minus 1. Losing with a roster that was projected to lose costs nothing. **Rebuild credit** (`gmRebuildWeight`, `gmProgressScore`): for a rebuilding team (0.45) and/or a patient GM (up to 0.35 more), that share of the score comes from progress instead of results: change in the value of his three best players 25 and under, extra first round picks, cap room and the win trend. Job security is the last three judged seasons weighted 1/0.6/0.35 (_Secure_, _Stable_, _Warm_, _Hot seat_). **Honeymoon:** no firing in his first two full seasons unless his job security is below -24, a season about 20+ wins under what his roster was projected to win; the earliest ordinary firing is after his third. Then the chance of being fired grows with how far below -9 that score is (-14 for a rebuilding team in his first four seasons, up to 3 more for a patient GM), shorter for win-now GMs hired into projected contenders (up to +4), ±1.5 by market size (big-market owners are less patient), plus droughts without the playoffs past 4 seasons (5 when rebuilding), from 20% up to 85%. GMs retire from 66 (certainly by 75). The **GM carousel** fills each job with the best of the former GMs on the market (fired or left within 4 seasons, 66 or younger, never back to a team he ran) and three new candidates, by career record plus noise. Fired GMs who stay unemployed 4 seasons retire.
- **Storage:** on the team objects: `t.gm` (current GM, with every season's record and expectation, tenures and job security) and `t.formerGms` (GMs who left that team, including retired ones without their faces). No database store, so league files keep them with the teams (`team.generate` passes them through). Code: `src/worker/core/gm/index.ts` (`getGmWeights`, `getAllGmWeights`, `ensureGms`, `onRegularSeason`, `runOffseason`), views `frontOffices.ts`, `gmShared.ts`.
- **Measured** (AI GM audit below, `AI_GMS=0` vs on, every other extra on, seeds 3/4/5 × 10 seasons, 30 teams; per-season means): AI-AI trades 12.8 → 14.5 per season (per-team totals over 10 seasons: mean 8.5 → 9.6, sd 3.0 → 3.8, so volume is a little higher and spread more by team; a team's count correlates only weakly, about 0.12, with its GM's trade frequency, since luck and needs dominate), teams over the tax 4.0 → 3.8, over the cap 15.8 → 15.1, mean contract 7.34% → 7.30% of the cap, win% sd 0.15 → 0.15, team ovr sd 13.7 → 13.4, strategy switches 2.9 → 3.2 per season, FA signings 36.9 → 38.4, re-signings 37.1 → 38.9, extensions 30.2 → 28.5. GMs: 2.2 fired and 0.2 retired per season, mean tenure about 6 seasons (capped by the 10 season runs). Fair job security (seeds 12/13/14 × 16 seasons, `AI_GM_DUMP`; before = the old rules, seeds 12/14): 2.7 → 2.0 fired per season; share of hires fired by team quality at hire (previous season win%, bottom/middle/top third) 47%/59%/38% → 29%/27%/50% (win-now hires on contenders have the shorter leash; few top-third hires); mean tenure of ended tenures 5.8 → 7.0 seasons (all tenures 6.4 → 7.3); fired within 2 seasons 4% → 9% of firings (only extreme misses), within 3 38% → 41%.
- **Known limits:** GMs don't make in-season coaching changes, have no contracts, and don't run the user's team when you take it over (his GM card is kept but idle). Leanings never change.

## Front office budget

- **Where:** Team → _Finances_ (the _Front office budget_ form replaces _Expense levels_, and _What your spending bought_ sits above Player Salaries), an _Analytics projection_ line in trade summaries for players you'd receive, a _Proj_ column on _Free Agents_, and the game sim, AI budgets and owner mood. Setting `featureFrontOffice` (basketball, on for new leagues, off for existing ones, off in college leagues). It needs the `budget` setting; with the budget off nothing changes.
- **Departments:** Scouting, Analytics (new), Medical (the health budget item), Travel & Sports Science (new), Coaching (set by the coaching staff when that's on) and Facilities. Each has a level from 1 to 100 like the old budget items, a slider with its league rank and the league average, its annual cost, and a one-line effect at the 3-season average level (and at the slider's level after 3 seasons). Analytics and Travel cost 0.4x a budget item's level (`DEPT_COST_SCALE`); their levels live in `t.frontOffice`, are paid each regular season game into `teamSeason.frontOffice.expenses` and recorded in `teamSeason.frontOffice.expenseLevels` like the others, and start (and are imputed for missing seasons) at the team's initial level, the average of its initial scouting, health and facilities levels, so a new level phases in over 3 seasons like the old items.
- **Owner envelope:** the owner funds every department at a target level by market size (`ownerTargetLevel`: popRank like `defaultBudgetLevel` without its random part, about 60 for the biggest market, 34 in the middle, 10 for the smallest). The bar shows the planned spend against it; more in one department means less in another unless you go over. Over the envelope (beyond 2%), end-of-season owner money mood drops 0.75 per 100% over (`overspendMoodPenalty`), on top of the lower profit. With the coaching staff on, coaching is its staff's salaries, which the budget doesn't set, so it is outside the envelope and the spending measured against it (before, the envelope counted coaching at the target level while the staff's payroll is about half that, so every team had about 10% of hidden headroom). Checked end to end (sweep19, a default league, the user's departments set before the season, played through the playoffs to the owner mood update at the end of the season, grace period ended, no auto play): every department at the owner's target (39), spend exactly the envelope, no penalty, money +0.49; every department at 100, 44% over the envelope, penalty 0.32 and money -0.12 (the rest of the drop is the lower profit, $83M -> $34M). The owner's profit expectation is lowered by what the two new departments cost at level 34, so owner mood is unchanged at average spending.
- **Effects** (`src/common/frontOffice.ts`; every one reads `levelToEffect` of the 3-season average, 0 at level 34, linear below and tanh above, so the top of the scale buys less than the bottom costs; all neutral at the league average):
  - _Scouting_: unchanged and reused — the scouting budget already sets the scouting department's size and quality (2-5 scouts, Scouting Reports) and prospect fuzz (stddev 3.1 at level 1, 2 at 34, 0.94 at 100). With [Draft Lottery Night & Combine](#draft-lottery-night-and-combine) on, a department at a 3-season level of 55 or more (`EARLY_COMBINE_LEVEL`) gets an early read of the current class's combine from the playoffs on (the prospect's Draft Combine section says _early read_; the combine is a pure function of each prospect, so it's exactly what the combine will show at the draft lottery; not while a linked college league's automatic import is still to replace the class, see [College mode](college-mode.md)).
  - _Analytics_: your projection of a player's ovr next season (an 80% band from an age curve, `analyticsProjection`) is 1.4x as wide at level 1 and 0.65x at 100, and a better department's center sees through that share of the scouting fuzz; with Coaching Systems the head coach's game management is ±12 points (`coachSimEffects` input, the league is centered again by `normalizeCoachSims`) and with Realistic Rotations his youth/veteran minutes bias ×0.6 at the top to ×1.45 at the bottom; with AI GM Personalities a GM whose team spends leans analytics (±0.3 on his trait, `getGmWeights`).
  - _Medical_: the health budget's injury durations are unchanged (-12% to +13%); Medical adds the in-game injury rate (×0.93 to ×1.08, `medicalInjuryFactor` on each player in the sim) and the recovery of players 30 and older (another -10% to +11% of the duration, fully from 34). Nothing is counted twice.
  - _Travel & Sports Science_: the endurance penalties of travel and 3 games in 4 nights (Load, Fatigue & Venue) and of back-to-backs (Injury Depth) ×0.53 at the top to ×1.5 at the bottom (`travelPenaltyScale`); with neither feature on, endurance ±1.5% instead.
- **AI teams** (preseason, `allocateAi`): the owner's spending (the usual `defaultBudgetLevel` by market size, with its randomness) split over the departments by the GM's leanings (`aiDeptWeights`, mean 1 so the total stays): analytics GMs spend on analytics and less on scouting, old-school GMs the other way, win-now GMs on medical and travel, patient ones on scouting and coaching. Without GMs the leanings come from the team strategy and a fixed per-team taste.
- **What your spending bought** (measured, `teamSeason.frontOffice.review`, compared with the same events at level 34): injury games saved = the duration difference of each injury from the same random draw at an average health budget, plus the injuries the Medical rate avoided (Σ 1/factor - 1) times their length; prospect rating error = your mean |fuzz| on the current prospects against the same draws scaled to an average scouting budget; travel fatigue saved = the endurance percentage points of travel, back-to-back and 3-in-4 penalties your sports science staff took off. Early in a season (under 10 games) last season's review is shown.
- **Off:** none of this runs: no extra random numbers, the old budget code paths (AI budget resets, injury durations, sim factors) are taken literally, and the outcome invariance test passes unchanged. A seeded headless league with the setting off gives the same fingerprint as master (see Calibration).
- **Calibration** (`RUN_FRONT_OFFICE_CALIBRATION=1`, `src/test/frontOffice/calibration.script.test.ts`: 8 headless leagues (seeds 1-8) × 2 seasons with the new-league defaults, every team AI-run; _avg_ sets every team's Scouting, Analytics, Medical, Travel and Facilities to 34, _ext_ is the same leagues with even teams at 100 and odd teams at 1, levels set every preseason with `initialBudget` so the 3-season averages are the set levels from the first game; 240 team-seasons per extreme, per 82 games):

  | Measure                                                | Level 1 (min) | Level 34 (avg) | Level 100 (max) |
  | ------------------------------------------------------ | ------------- | -------------- | --------------- |
  | In-game injuries per team                              | 16.95         | 15.83          | 14.57           |
  | Injury games missed per team                           | 137.7         | 117.3          | 92.7            |
  | Measured injury games saved (season review)            | -26.9         | 0              | +24.2           |
  | Prospect fuzz, mean \|fuzz\| of a fresh draw (stddev)  | 2.47 (3.10)   | 1.58 (2.00)    | 0.61 (0.94)     |
  | Scouts in the scouting department                      | 2             | 3              | 5               |
  | Endurance penalty per tired game (travel, b2b, 3-in-4) | 4.28%         | 2.88%          | 1.53%           |
  | Share of that penalty taken off                        | -50%          | 0%             | +48%            |
  | Wins (paired with the same team at 34)                 | -2.37 ± 0.81  | 41.0           | +2.37 ± 0.79    |
  | Point differential (paired)                            | -1.31 ± 0.46  | 0              | +1.31 ± 0.43    |

  The win effect is zero-sum within a league (half the teams gain what the other half lose); by point differential it's about ±3.5 wins. League-wide the _ext_ leagues match the _avg_ ones (15.76 vs 15.83 injuries, 115 vs 117 injury games, 2.90% vs 2.88% fatigue per tired game), and with the AI's own allocation (_natural_) every department averages 37 (sd 21, like the old budget items, whose AI default also averages 37), with 15.93 injuries, 119.8 injury games and 2.86% fatigue: neutral within noise. Prospect fuzz is the scouting budget's existing formula (sampled), younger classes multiply it by up to 2.

- **Off is unchanged:** `src/test/frontOffice/offIdentity.script.test.ts` (two seeded headless leagues, a season and the next preseason with the setting off, every other new-league default on): the `simFingerprint` hash and the next random number match master exactly (ea8ef5d5… and f662b216… for seeds 11 and 12 on both).
- **Code:** `src/common/frontOffice.ts` (formulas), `src/worker/core/frontOffice/index.ts` (levels, payments, review, AI allocation), hooks in `game/writeTeamStats.ts`, `game/writePlayerStats.ts`, `player/injury.ts`, `game/loadTeams.ts`, `game/injuryDepthTeam.ts`, `GameSim.basketball/index.ts`, `staff/systems.ts`, `gm/index.ts`, `phase/newPhasePreseason.ts`, `season/updateOwnerMood.ts`, views `teamFinances.ts`, `freeAgents.ts`, `trade/summary.ts`, UI `src/ui/views/TeamFinances/FrontOffice.tsx`. Tests: `src/common/frontOffice.test.ts`, `src/worker/core/frontOffice/frontOffice.test.ts`; calibration `src/test/frontOffice/calibration.script.test.ts`.
- **Known limits:** the scouting review approximates the level the fuzz was drawn at by the current 3-season level. The Expenses bar graph doesn't show the two new departments separately (they're in the cash and profit). The rotation effect needs Coaching Systems and Realistic Rotations.

## Realistic AI economy

Setting `featureRealisticEconomy` (_Realistic AI Economy_, basketball, on for new leagues, off for existing ones, off in college leagues). Off = stock BBGM exactly (none of the code below runs, and no extra random draws).

- **Fair random AI-to-AI trades:** the classic random AI trade (`betweenAiTeams` `attempt`) lets the initiating team lose up to 15 value units (stock `|dv2| > 15` check). On: the initiating team's value change must be at least `FAIR_TRADE_MIN_DV` (-2) (the other side already gains, from `makeItWork`); a rejected attempt is retried with new players up to `FAIR_TRADE_RETRIES` (2) times, so trade volume stays close to stock.
- **Soft salary matching without a cap:** with `salaryCapType` none, a team over the luxury tax after an AI-to-AI trade (random or need-based two-team) can't take back more than 125% + $100k of the salary it sends out (`trade/realisticEconomy.ts`). User trades are unchanged.
- **Owner distributions:** at the end of the regular season (with the luxury tax), each AI team's owner takes 75% of the cash above one salary cap (`finances/ownerDistribution.ts`; a news item, no notification). Cash is not an input to anything else (owner mood reads profit), so it only keeps the cash column realistic. User teams are left alone (in spectator mode or auto play every team is AI).
- **Measured** (`economyAudit.script.test.ts`, `RUN_ECO_AUDIT=1`, seed 1, 15 seasons, `ECO_SET='{"featureRealisticEconomy":false|true}'`), cash in $M, payroll in salary caps:

  | Config  | Season | Cash median off -> on | Cash max off -> on | Payroll max off -> on |
  | ------- | ------ | --------------------- | ------------------ | --------------------- |
  | default | 1      | 24 -> 20              | 171 -> 164         | 1.55 -> 1.55          |
  | default | 5      | 149 -> 161            | 1,072 -> 281       | 1.44 -> 1.33          |
  | default | 10     | 712 -> 179            | 2,325 -> 293       | 1.55 -> 1.32          |
  | default | 15     | 1,164 -> 193          | 3,328 -> 274       | 1.35 -> 1.38          |
  | no cap  | 1      | 31 -> 23              | 132 -> 197         | 1.49 -> 1.49          |
  | no cap  | 5      | 172 -> 155            | 795 -> 244         | 1.93 -> 1.14          |
  | no cap  | 10     | 299 -> 181            | 1,373 -> 282       | 1.49 -> 1.36          |
  | no cap  | 15     | 612 -> 187            | 2,300 -> 278       | 1.48 -> 1.38          |

  15-season means, off -> on: default trades/season 15.0 -> 16.1, payroll max 1.39 -> 1.32 caps, teams over the tax 6.5 -> 6.5; no cap trades/season 53.9 -> 35.7 (the salary rule blocks the payroll-ballooning ones), payroll max 1.55 -> 1.27, teams over the tax 8.9 -> 4.1, trades the audit scores as losing for a side 2.9 -> 0.9. 0 invalid states either way.

## Downloads

Works in every sport.

- **Tables as CSV.** DataTables (most tables in the game, native or not) keep the stock _Download spreadsheet_ item in their ⋮ menu. The fork's other tables and lists get a **Download CSV** menu at the top right of the page that lists every such table on screen, named by its caption or nearest heading (Draft Board, Mock Draft, College/Overseas, Coaches, Records Book, Milestones, Rivalries, Series Recaps, Award Vote, Newspaper, Traits, Storylines, ...). Card lists opt in with `data-csv-table` (`data-csv-cols` for the header, `data-csv-row` on each item: Best Games, Top Plays, Timeline, Games to Watch, Trade Offers, and the Wrapped deck, one row per slide, where a quiz slide's answer is only included once you answered it). The file is read from the rendered page, so it has exactly what you see: scouting fuzz, trait ranges and the no visible ratings challenge apply, and the secret aging curve never shows before retirement. The page list is `NATIVE_CSV_PAGES` in `src/ui/components/TablesCSV.tsx`; `data-no-csv` keeps a table out. Code: `src/ui/util/tableCSV.ts`.
- **Download players.** Every player table (roster, free agents, draft scouting/draft, player ratings and stats, trade pages, upcoming free agents, watch list, ...) has _Download players (PDF/JSON/CSV)_ in its ⋮ menu: the players the table shows with its filters, search and sort (all pages of it), for the table's season. The single player page has a _Download player_ button.
  - **PDF**: each player starts on a new page and gets as many landscape pages as his profile needs: overview (face, bio, contract, archetype, ratings with bars, skills and badges), stats (every stats table of the player page, regular season and playoffs, with career rows), ratings history, traits by category with status lines (aging curve only for retired players), college/overseas stats and honors, awards. Long tables continue across pages with their header repeated; tables too wide for a page are split into column groups that repeat Year/Team/Age. Each page has the player's name and "page x of y". Optionally (off by default) a section with this season's game log, and optionally a zip with one PDF per player instead of one PDF (`src/ui/util/zip.ts`, stored). Written by a small built-in PDF writer (`src/ui/util/miniPdf.ts`, JPEG faces) with an embedded Unicode font: `public/fonts/DejaVuSans*-latin.ttf` (DejaVu Sans, free license in `DejaVuSans-LICENSE.txt`), subsets of about 50 KB per weight covering Latin, Latin Extended A/B, Latin Extended Additional (Vietnamese) and combining marks, so names like Dončić, Şengün, Łukasz, Kėdainis, Þórsson or Nguyễn come out right and the text can be searched and copied. The fonts are fetched the first time a PDF is made (and precached by the service worker); without them the PDF falls back to Helvetica with accents dropped. Remake the subsets with `pyftsubset` (fontTools) from DejaVuSans.ttf / DejaVuSans-Bold.ttf with `--unicodes="U+0020-007E,U+00A0-024F,U+0259,U+02BB-02BC,U+02C6-02DD,U+0300-036F,U+1E00-1EFF,U+2010-2027,U+2030-203A,U+20AC,U+2122,U+2212" --layout-features='' --no-hinting --drop-tables+=kern,GSUB,GPOS,GDEF,FFTM,MATH,gasp`. The layout code is lazy loaded (`src/ui/util/playersPdf.ts`).
  - **JSON**: one readable file (`format: "zengm-visible-players"`) with the same data (game log too when ticked). For moving players into another league with exact ratings, the dialog links to the stock _Export Players_.
  - **CSV**: one row per player: bio, contract, scouted ratings, skills/badges, archetype, trait readings, season and career summary stats.
  - The worker (`src/worker/util/playerExport.ts`) gathers what the user can see, 20 players per request with a progress bar, so a big selection doesn't freeze the page: fuzzed ratings, no ratings at all under the no visible ratings challenge (except retired players, like the player page), the user's trait readings (ranges for prospects), and the aging curve only once revealed. Data shaping and CSV escaping (`src/common/playerExport.ts`) are tested in `src/common/playerExport.test.ts`.
- **Export profile.** Every player page (Overview, Game Log, College/Overseas, Traits, Scouting) has an _Export profile_ button next to _Edit_ (also _Export profile (every page)..._ in the Overview's _Download player_ menu). A small dialog picks the format (JSON and PDFs in one zip, JSON only, or the PDF set) and the sections; sections that don't apply to him are greyed out with the reason. Works for active players, free agents, prospects and retired players, in pro and college leagues, and at phone width.
  - **Sections:** Overview (bio, team, draft or class year, contract, injury, archetype, ovr/pot, skills, badges, season and career stat lines, note, relatives, feats, league leader seasons, contract value index), Ratings (current and history, scouted), Stats (every stats table of the Overview, regular season and playoffs, game highs), Game Log (his latest season with games, the seasons that have one, team record in his games), College/Overseas (stats tables, honors, draft or projected pick, combine with percentiles, and the page's whole data: recruiting, college season, splits, stock, comparisons, story, scouting report), Traits (every reading with its category and status, what known traits mean in games, the known traits by season, the aging curve only once retired), Scouting (the report as the user's scouts write it: grades with notes, header, breakdowns, flags, pro report, development, team fit, the two scouts' takes, stock up/down, track record, the scouting department), Awards and honors (awards, honors before the draft, jersey numbers and retirements), Contract and mood (contract or asking amount, willingness, chance to sign, mood traits and components, salaries, career earnings; not for retired players, prospects or college leagues), Transactions (draft, signings, trades, releases), Injuries (current, history, by body part, injury prone, minutes restriction).
  - **JSON** (`<pid>_<name>_profile.json`, no byte order mark): `format: "zengm-player-profile"`, `schemaVersion: 1` (bumped when a field changes meaning; added fields don't bump it), `exportedAt`, the league context (`lid`, name, sport, season, phase and phase name, college or not, starting season, the user's team), `visibility` (ratings shown or not, the challenge, Player Traits, Scouting Reports), the `player` (pid, name, status `active`/`freeAgent`/`prospect`/`retired`, team, position, age, born, height, weight, college, draft, class year in college leagues, jersey, experience), `skipped` (requested sections that don't apply, with the reason) and `sections`: each has `title`, `note`, `facts` (label/value), `lines`, `tables` (`cols`/`rows`, like the PDF) and `data` (the section's structured data).
  - **PDF set**: a zip with a folder of one PDF per section (`01_overview.pdf`, `02_ratings.pdf`, ... `11_injuries.pdf`), landscape like _Download players_ and drawn by the same code: the overview card (face, bio, ratings bars) and the scouting card (grade bars, radar, shot mix, comparisons, report) followed by the section's facts and tables, each page with his name, the section and "page x of y". Tables wrap their last column (trait status, events) instead of cutting it. Team colors too light for paper (white, yellow) fall back to navy.
  - **Hidden information:** built from the same worker views as the pages, so the pages' rules apply: scouted (fuzzed) ratings, none at all under the no visible ratings challenge except for retired players (rating keys are also stripped from anything taken from a page, like a league leader season in a rating), trait readings as ranges while unknown with no score, the aging curve only once retired, the scouting report from the user's department. The raw player never leaves the worker; the report's internal takes are left out.
  - **Code:** `src/common/playerProfileExport.ts` (format, sections, helpers), `src/worker/util/playerProfileExport.ts` (`getPlayerProfileExport`, `getPlayerProfileExportSections`), `src/ui/components/ExportProfile.tsx` (dialog, files), `src/ui/util/profilePdf.ts` (PDF set), tested in `src/worker/util/playerProfileExport.test.ts` (sections per kind of player, scouted ratings and no leaks, the challenge, college leagues, a PDF and zip smoke test).

## Known limits (all features)

- Basketball only unless a section says otherwise; the other sports hide the menu items and links.
- The Wrapped, Storylines and Timeline pages share a whole-history snapshot that takes a while the first time in a big league.
- The league-wide averages with player traits, the possession model or injury depth on stay about the same as with them off, but individual games and seasons are of course different; use the switches (off) to replay the old behaviour.
- Existing leagues keep playing as before until the game-affecting switches (possession model, traits, injury depth, contracts, trades, drafting, coaching staff, GM personalities) are turned on in League Extras. Games already played replay unchanged.
- Draft board and scouting need prospects: the Draft Board is empty in a league without upcoming classes.

## Performance

Wrapped and Storylines read the whole league into a snapshot. Converted pieces that can't change any more (retired players, finished seasons' box scores and team seasons) are cached in a separate IndexedDB database (`wrapped-cache`), versioned by the converter code and invalidated through the league's change log, so later sessions only read what changed. Idle snapshots are evicted from memory. `tools/perf/wrapped/` holds the Playwright benchmark scripts (see the header of `bench.cjs`).

## Testing

- Unit tests: `node_modules/.bin/vitest --run --project basketball` (plus `football` and `baseball`, which must stay green since the features are gated).
- Test isolation: files share a worker (`isolate: false`). A league made with `league.createStream` starts its cache's automatic flushes; a cache replaced without stopping them (`resetCache` in a later test) used to keep flushing its stale records into whatever league was open next (an unhandled "objectStore.put is not a function" against a mock league, a ConstraintError against a real one). `resetCache` now stops the old cache, and a cache that is no longer `idb.cache` never flushes again (`Cache._autoFlush`).
- Sim calibration tables (not part of the normal run): possession model `POSS_METRICS=1`, player traits `TRAITS_METRICS=1`, coaching systems `COACH_CAL=1`, spacing & shot creation and matchup defense `SM_CAL=1` (`spacingMatchup.script.test.ts`) (see the test files' headers).
- Long-run league check (not part of the normal run): `LONG_RUN=1 LONG_RUN_CONFIG=defaults|all|off|random LONG_RUN_SEASONS=20 LONG_RUN_OUT=/tmp/run.jsonl node_modules/.bin/vitest run --project basketball src/worker/core/league/longRun.script.test.ts` plays a spectator league season by season and writes one JSON line per season (league averages, ratings levels and age curve, positions and archetypes, rookie quality, player counts, roster sizes, salaries, sim and offseason CPU time with a fixed-work calibration, database and cache size) plus `PROBLEM` lines from its checks of every box score (points, minutes with overtime, team totals, plus/minus, fouls, starters, quarters), season totals against the box scores, advanced stats ranges, awards (winners, rookies, duplicates), the playoff bracket against the box scores, the All-Star MVP and the Records Book's top marks. `LONG_RUN_RANDOM_GAME=1` (with `random`) also randomizes the game rules (periods, quarter and overtime length, Elam ending, foul-outs, three pointers, players on the court, overtimes and shootouts, playoff format); `LONG_RUN_SETTINGS` adds settings, `LONG_RUN_FORCEWIN=1` forces God Mode results, `LONG_RUN_PROF=prefix` writes a CPU profile of the last offseason.
- AI GM audit (not part of the normal run; `AI_GMS=0` turns GM personalities off for a before/after, and with them on it also logs GM firings/hires, strategy switches and per-team trade counts): `RUN_AI_AUDIT=1 AI_SEEDS=1,2 AI_SEASONS=4 node_modules/.bin/vitest --run --project basketball src/test/ratingInflation/aiAudit.script.test.ts` simulates a league with every extra on and writes one JSON line per season (`AI_OUT`, default `/tmp/aiAudit.jsonl`): AI-AI trades (three-team, with pick conditions, for trade requests), free agent signings, re-signings, extensions, trade requests (and how many were traded), holdouts, teams over the tax/cap, dead money, roster sizes, win% and team ovr spread, mean contract (% of cap), mean ovr, and the offers AI teams would send the user right now, valued from the user's side. `AI_CAPEX=0` turns salary cap exceptions off, `AI_REPEATER=1` / `AI_STRETCH=1` turn on their options; it also logs free agent signings above the minimum, their mean and total salary, exception uses by type, stretched releases, luxury tax paid and repeater taxpayers.
- Economy audit (not part of the normal run): `RUN_ECO_AUDIT=1 ECO_CONFIG=default ECO_SEASONS=15 node_modules/.bin/vitest --run --project basketball src/test/ratingInflation/economyAudit.script.test.ts`, one long league per run (configs `default`, `hard`, `none`, `repeater` (repeater tax + stretch), `small` (16 teams), `large` (40), `expansion` (2 teams added in season 5), `noGms`; `ECO_BASE=defaults` uses only the new-league defaults, lighter than every extra on). Each season: payrolls against the cap, tax and aprons, roster sizes, contracts against a value-based reference, unsigned good free agents, AI-AI trades valued by both teams' own trade AI right after they're made (`ECO_TRADE_DUMP=file` lists the lopsided ones), free agency, cap exceptions, GM and head coach changes, profit, cash and the owner's money mood, front office levels and spending against the envelope, rumor calibration, and invalid states (players without contracts, stale or duplicate picks, conditions owed to folded teams, hard cap overages). `firedUser.script.test.ts` (`RUN_FIRED_USER=1`): the owner fires the user after a season, he takes another team and plays on.
- Lint: `node_modules/.bin/eslint .` and `node_modules/.bin/tsc --build`.
